### Bulk score entry: CSV template download/upload per class and subject (2026-07-12)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-12
- Notes: user directive: "add the bulk option of enter scores for each class, subject and students. the teacher should be able to download the template that has the names, subjects... fill it and upload it back and it saves automatically... a teacher can determine to do bulk upload for just one subject also." `ExaminationController::bulkTemplate()` exports a CSV pre-filled with every student's admission number/name plus one column per (subject, assessment component) pair the teacher is allowed to enter for that class arm, existing scores already filled in so it doubles as an export. Column header format is `"{Subject}: {Component}"` (colon, not a dash, per the standing no-dashes-in-on-screen-text rule since this header is genuinely user facing and opens directly in Excel). `bulkUpload()` re-reads that same shape: a blank cell is skipped, never treated as a zero; every entered value is checked numeric and within the component's real `max_score`, an out of range cell is skipped and reported but valid cells in the same upload still save. `bulkSubjectIds()` resolves either one subject (if `subject_id` is passed) or every subject the teacher teaches in that class arm (if not), covering the "single subject or all at once" requirement from one code path.
- Issues/Bugs found during build (fixed before shipping, not live bugs): a test initially asserted unquoted CSV output but `fputcsv` quotes any field containing a comma-adjacent context by its own rules, fixed the assertion to match real output rather than assumed output; `streamedContent()` was called on a plain `Response` (only exists on `StreamedResponse`), fixed test calls to use `getContent()`.
- Resolution: n/a, no post-ship defects found.
- QA: 6 new tests in `ExaminationScreenTest` (template pre-fill, upload saves and skips blanks, out-of-range cells rejected while valid ones still save, a multi-subject teacher's template covers every subject when none is specified, single-subject template filtering, forbidden for a class not taught). All passing as part of the full suite.

### Comment Bank and configurable multi-stage report card review workflow (2026-07-12)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-12
- Notes: built from two uploaded docs, "Proposed Comments Architecture.docx" and "Intelligent Assessment & Report Card Workflow.docx", user directive: "develop everything needed for the report card, the grading system and also populate each student with live scores, comments etc (leave some for us to review the workflow, approval etc)", later confirmed to cover the full workflow too ("also confrm if you are buulding the workflows in the documents too, I need it done too") and to include the Locked/Archived statuses originally going to be staged out ("build all the locked or defered item here so that this build is complete for now"). True AI-generated comments stay out, no AI provider is configured anywhere in the app, tracked at section 5c.
  1. **Comment Bank, DB-backed** (user correction: "note that i dont want anything hardcoded as you know", reinforcing the standing "DB-backed layer" rule added to CLAUDE.md this same pass): new tenant `comments` table (`min_score`, `max_score`, `text`, `is_active`) and `App\Models\Tenant\Comment`. `App\Services\Results\CommentBank::ensureSeeded()` lazily seeds the table from `config('skulos.default_comment_bank')` (100 real comments across 5 score bands, sourced verbatim from the docx) the first time it is empty, a no-op after, matching the same seed-once-then-DB-is-truth pattern `grading_scales`/`grading_bands` already established. `suggestionFor(float $score)` queries the DB table by range, never reads `config()` directly at runtime. Wired into the Results (`/examinations`) screen: a "Remark" column per student, pre-filled with a saved `SubjectRemark` if one exists, otherwise a grey suggested comment from the bank a teacher can accept or overwrite.
  2. **SubjectRemark** (new tenant model/table): a per (student, subject, term) teacher-authored or accepted remark, distinct from `Score` (per assessment component) and the existing `StudentTermRemark` (per term overall remark, class teacher/principal level).
  3. **Configurable multi-stage review chain** (user directive: "Admin configures Workflow... Entirely configurable", e.g. Teacher to Class Teacher to VP Academic to Principal at one school, Teacher to Principal at another): `ReportCardReview::reviewChain()` reads tenant Setting `report_card_review_chain` (an ordered array of role slugs from `config('skulos.report_card_review_roles')`) or falls back to `config('skulos.default_report_card_review_chain')` (`['class-teacher']`, reproducing the original single-stage behaviour exactly, so no existing school changes behaviour until it deliberately configures a chain). New `review_stage` column tracks position in the chain. `ReportCardController::approve()` now advances `review_stage` and stays `pending_review` at any non-final stage, only becoming `approved` at the final stage, exactly matching the pre-existing single-stage behaviour when the chain has one entry. `sendBack()` resets `review_stage` to 0, a correction restarts the whole chain. New `updateReviewChain()` action plus a chain-builder UI on the Report Settings page (`school/report-cards/settings.blade.php`, 5 stage dropdowns, stage 1 required, "Not used" truncates the chain there).
  4. **Promotion recommendation** (user directive from the docx: "Class Teacher completes Promotion: Promote / Repeat / Conditional Promotion, Reason"): new `promotion_recommendation`/`promotion_reason` columns, `ReportCardReview::PROMOTION_PROMOTE`/`PROMOTION_REPEAT`/`PROMOTION_CONDITIONAL` constants, new `updatePromotion()` action, a dropdown on the class-index page. Not stage gated, any reviewer along the chain can set or change it.
  5. **Locked and Archived statuses** (docx flow: "...Approve -> Result Locked -> Publish -> Parents View -> Archive"): `STATUS_LOCKED` (an explicit, deliberate "truly final" step past Released, only reachable from Released) and `STATUS_ARCHIVED` (reachable from Released or Locked, takes a report card out of active lists once its term is over) added to `ReportCardReview`, with `lock()`/`archive()` controller actions and buttons on the class-index page. `LOCKED_FOR_EDITING` (`[approved, locked, released, archived]`) is now enforced in `ExaminationController::save()` and `bulkUpload()`: a student whose report card review is past Approved has their score edits silently skipped (reported back in the flash message, e.g. "1 student(s) skipped because their report card is locked for editing"), a reviewer must explicitly send it back to `needs_correction` first, this is the actual teeth behind "Locked."
- Issues/Bugs found: none live, this was a net-new build on top of already-tested single-stage behaviour, the existing 23 `ReportCardWorkflowTest`/`ReportCardTest` tests were re-run unmodified after every change specifically to catch any regression to the original single-stage school and passed throughout.
- Resolution: n/a.
- QA: `php artisan tenants:migrate` run and confirmed against every real provisioned school (`comments`/`subject_remarks` tables and the 5 new `report_card_reviews` columns verified present on `greenfield`'s actual tenant database via tinker, not just the test suite's fresh sqlite databases). 12 new tests added (two-stage chain requires two approvals, unknown role rejected, send-back resets stage, promotion recommendation saves, lock requires released first, archive accepts released or locked, draft cannot be archived, settings page renders the chain builder, a locked report card's score edits are skipped while an unrelated student's still save). Full suite: 409 passed, 0 failed, run clean after all changes landed, no regressions.
- Not built in this pass: populating every real student with live scores and comments across schools (explicitly requested next, deliberately left for a following pass so the review/approval workflow above stays untouched for the user to test manually first).

### Live bug: a new school's subdomain 404'd, mixed-case slug corrupted its database name (2026-07-12)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-12
- Notes: user report, live: "I create a new school and the school subdomain is not accessible, 404... this must not repeat itself in localhost and when we go live." Root cause traced to a real school created earlier the same session (id 47, "Training"): `SchoolAdminController::store()`'s optional manual slug field is only validated `alpha_dash` (which allows uppercase), and the code only ran `Str::slug()` over the school's *name* when no slug was typed (`$slug = $data['slug'] ?? Str::slug($data['name'])`), so a typed value like "Training" was stored verbatim, capital T and all. Two knock-on failures from that one bad value: (1) `ResolveTenant`'s lookup was an exact `where('slug', $slug)` match against whatever the browser sent as the host, always lowercase in practice, so `training.<domain>` never matched a stored slug of `Training`, a real 404; (2) `TenantManager::databaseNameFor()` sanitizes a slug to `[a-z0-9_]` via `replaceMatches`, but never lowercases first, so the uppercase "T" was silently *deleted* rather than case-folded, leaving the school's real tenant database physically named `raining` instead of `training`, a plainly wrong name that Comment Bank/report card build had no reason to ever surface.
- Issues/Bugs found: exactly as above, plus the same missing-normalization pattern in `SchoolAdminController::cloneSchool()`'s slug handling (not yet hit live, fixed alongside since it is the same bug in a second location).
- Resolution: three layers, so this fails closed at every point rather than depending on one guard holding. 1) `SchoolAdminController::store()`/`cloneSchool()` now always run `Str::slug($data['slug'] ?? $data['name'])`, so a typed slug is normalized exactly the same as an auto-derived one, never stored raw. 2) `TenantManager::databaseNameFor()` now lowercases before stripping invalid characters, so even a stray mixed-case slug from any other future code path degrades to a correct lowercase database name instead of silently losing letters. 3) `ResolveTenant`'s school lookup is now case-insensitive (`whereRaw('LOWER(slug) = ?', ...)`), so a slug that is somehow still mixed-case (a direct DB edit, a future admin rename feature) still resolves rather than 404ing, the case-insensitive read is a safety net independent of the write-side fix. The one already-broken real school (id 47) was repaired directly: its tenant sqlite file was copied to the correctly spelled `training.sqlite` (verified readable via a throwaway DB connection before touching anything), the `School` row's `slug`/`database` columns updated to `training`, the old `raining.sqlite` removed only after the copy was confirmed intact. No other of the 7 real provisioned schools had a mixed-case slug (checked via a full scan), this was an isolated, single-school incident, not a systemic data problem.
- QA: confirmed live against the real dev server (port 8000, left running throughout, never restarted) that `training.<domain>` now redirects to its login page instead of 404ing. Two new regression tests in `SchoolAdminManagementTest` (a typed mixed-case slug stores lowercased with a matching lowercase database name and `isProvisioned()` true; a school created with no slug at all still derives a correctly lowercased one from its name) and one in `TenancyTest` (the tenant-resolution middleware accepts a mixed-case header/host and still matches a lowercase stored slug). Full suite: 413 passed, 0 failed, no regressions.

### Class order and structure: canonical Nursery/Primary/Junior/Senior ordering everywhere, subjects follow curriculum order not alphabetical (2026-07-12)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-12
- Notes: user directive, with screenshots and "Class Order & Structure.docx" as the reference: "the order is wrong, it ought to start from Nursery and Primary, Junior Secondary and then Senior Secondary... same way the list of subject should follow the order and not scatter or arrange alphabetically. Correct this across all areas." `ClassArm::orderKey()` (built 2026-07-11) already combined Section.sequence and SchoolClass.sequence correctly, existing dropdowns using it were not the bug, so the defect had to be upstream, in how those sequence numbers get assigned in the first place.
  1. **Root cause, Section ordering**: `AcademicDefaults::applyClassStructure()` assigned a newly created Section's sequence as `Section::max('sequence') + 1`, i.e. "whichever tier an admin ticks (or the seeder applies) first gets position 1," not the tier's real pedagogical position. A school that loaded Primary before Nursery permanently had Primary sort first everywhere sections are listed. Fixed by giving each tier in `config('skulos.default_class_structure')` a fixed `sequence` (Nursery 1, Primary 2, Secondary 3), used instead of `max()+1`, and corrected on every call (not just at creation), so re-applying a tier also fixes a school whose sequence was already wrong.
  2. **Root cause, class-within-section collision**: `jss` and `ss` share one "Secondary" section by design, but each tier's classes were sequenced `$index + 1` independently, so JSS 1-3 and SSS 1-3 both landed on sequence 1, 2, 3, a direct collision that could interleave them depending on tie-breaking. Fixed with a `class_base` per tier (0/10/20/30, spaced for headroom), so a class's sequence is `class_base + index + 1`, guaranteeing JSS always sorts before SSS regardless of application order.
  3. **Subjects had no ordering concept at all**: `Subject` had no sequence column, `AcademicsController::subjects()`'s per-class subject list was explicitly `orderBy('name')`, alphabetical, exactly the "scatter" the user reported. New `sequence` column (migration `2026_07_12_000006_add_sequence_to_subjects.php`), set from each `default_subjects` catalog's declared array order offset by a new per-catalog `sequence_base` (0/100/200/300) in `AcademicDefaults::applySubjectCatalog()`, only ever filling in a still-unset (0) sequence so a subject shared across catalogs or already manually reordered is never silently reset. `SchoolClass::subjects()`'s relation itself now carries `orderBy('subjects.sequence')`, so every caller across the app (report cards, examinations, the class subjects page, dashboards) gets the right order automatically without needing to remember to sort.
  4. **Missing manual-reorder capability, a real gap independent of the bug**: a class already had a `sequence` field and an `updateClass()` action (2026-07-11), but no form on any page ever exposed a "Position" input, so it was never actually admin-editable in practice; Sections had no rename/reorder action (`storeSection()` existed, nothing to change one afterwards) at all. Added a "Position" field to the class rename form (`class-show.blade.php`), a new `updateSection()` action + route + inline rename/reorder form per section card on the Classes & Subjects index, and a "Position" field on `updateSubject()`'s per-subject row form, so a school whose section/class/subject names do not match the built-in catalog (a fully custom curriculum) can still put everything in the right order themselves, not just schools using the default names.
  5. **Retroactive repair, creation-free by design**: `applyClassStructure()` cannot safely be re-run broadly to fix an already-provisioned real school, it would `firstOrCreate` an entire tier that school never opted into (inventing a Senior Secondary section for a primary-only school, for example). New `AcademicDefaults::realignSequences()` instead only corrects a Section/SchoolClass/Subject that already exists and matches a catalog name exactly, never creates anything; run once via tinker against all 7 real provisioned schools (`qa-academy`, `greenfield`, `test`, `test2`, `skultest`, `training`, and the archived `data-s`), correcting between 6 and 14 classes and 19 to 49 subjects per school (2 sections also needed correcting, on `greenfield`).
- Issues/Bugs found: all four described above (Section creation-order bug, JSS/SSS sequence collision, no subject ordering concept, no section rename/reorder UI and an unexposed class Position field). All fixed in this pass, no partial fixes left outstanding.
- Resolution: see above per item.
- QA: 6 new tests in `AcademicsScreenTest` (SSS classes sort strictly after JSS classes in the shared section; Section order is canonical regardless of which tier is loaded first; a class's subjects follow curriculum order, asserting "English Studies" first rather than alphabetical "Basic Digital Literacy"; `realignSequences()` proven to never invent a tier a school never loaded, only correcting an existing Primary section's wrong sequence; an admin can rename and reorder a section; an admin can manually reorder a subject within a class). Full suite: 419 passed, 0 failed (run in the background during this pass, confirmed clean before commit). Live verified against the real `greenfield` school's tenant database directly after running the retroactive repair: `Section::orderBy('sequence')` now reads Nursery (1), Primary (2), Secondary (3); the Secondary section's classes read JSS 1-3 (sequence 21-23) then SSS 1-3 (sequence 31-33), no more interleaving; Primary 1's subjects read English Studies, Mathematics, Nigerian Language... in the catalog's real order, not alphabetically. `php artisan tenants:migrate` run and confirmed against every real school for the new `subjects.sequence` column before the repair pass.
- Not built in this pass: splitting "Secondary" into separate Junior/Senior sections (the docx's full 5-section model), this remains a deliberate existing design decision from 2026-07-10 ("most schools run junior and senior secondary as one section, not two"), not something this pass was asked to revisit, the fix here corrects ordering within the existing 3-section (Nursery/Primary/Secondary) shape.

### Houses: leadership, sponsors, and staff/parent membership (2026-07-12)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-12
- Notes: user directive, live screenshot of a house page with no leadership or sponsor concept at all: "we should have the house master and its assistant selected from list of teachers too... let's have the option of selecting from parents as House Sponsor(s) (we can have more than one sponsor) - also, one can type the name of sponsor if the sponsor is not a parent... all Members of the house should be grouped into three 1, Students, 2. teachers and 3. parents... The list should be collapsible... Share all staff and parents into the houses under each school." House Master already existed (`housemaster_staff_id`) but had no UI; staff/parent membership did not exist at all, `HouseController::show()`'s own comment from 2026-07-10 explicitly deferred staff ("the Staff module itself has no admin screens or even a house_id column yet"), a gap since closed by the Staff module build.
  1. **Schema**: migration `2026_07_12_000007_...` adds `assistant_housemaster_staff_id` to `houses`, `house_id` to `staff` (mirrors `students.house_id` exactly), and `house_id` to `student_guardians` (a guardian's own per-student record, since there is no standalone deduplicated Guardian entity, a shared parent across siblings has one house-assignable row per child). Migration `2026_07_12_000008_...` creates `house_sponsors` (`house_id`, `student_guardian_id` nullable, `name` nullable), either/or enforced in the controller, not the DB.
  2. **Leadership**: new `updateLeadership()` action, a two-select form (House Master, Assistant House Master) on the house page, both sourced from `Staff::active()`, both independently optional and changeable.
  3. **Sponsors**: new `addSponsor()`/`removeSponsor()` actions and `HouseSponsor` model. `displayName()` resolves to the linked guardian's name if `student_guardian_id` is set, otherwise the free typed `name`. A house can have any number of sponsors.
  4. **Members grouped into three collapsible sections**: `house/show.blade.php` rebuilt with three `<details class="subject-disclosure">` blocks (reusing the existing collapsible-table CSS from the subject-teacher Students page build, 2026-07-12 earlier the same day, not a new pattern), Students (unchanged), Teachers (`Staff::where('house_id', ...)`), Parents (`StudentGuardian::where('house_id', ...)`). Each of Teachers/Parents gets an inline per-row house select (`assignStaff()`/`assignParent()`, one shared endpoint per type regardless of which house is currently viewed, so the same select works for both "add to this house" and "move to a different house") and an "Add existing [staff/parent] to this house" picker restricted to whoever is not already in a house.
  5. **"Share all staff and parents into the houses under each school", then extended to "add student to it too"**: new `distributeMembers()` bulk action, round robins every `Student`/`Staff`/`StudentGuardian` row with `house_id IS NULL` evenly across the school's *active* houses only (a retired house never receives a new member), leaving anyone already assigned untouched so it is safe to re-run after new staff or students join. Exposed as a button on the Houses index page, and also run once via tinker against every real provisioned school this same pass (`qa-academy` 74 staff/300 parents, `greenfield` 76/320, `test` 74/375, `test2` 74/300, `skultest` 74/300, `data-s` 74/300, `training` 0/0 since it has no staff or students yet; a second student-only pass placed 0 new students everywhere, confirming every real school's students already had a house from the earlier demo seeding, the addition is a safe no-op there and only matters for a school with unassigned students). Confirmed on `greenfield` afterward that every one of its 5 houses ended up with a real, roughly even spread (57 to 70 students, 15 to 16 staff, 64 parents each).
  6. **CSV import/export for staff and parents** (user directive 2026-07-12: "remember to add the import and export feature under the house section too"): new `staffBulkTemplate()`/`staffBulkAssign()` and `parentsBulkTemplate()`/`parentsBulkAssign()`, the exact same "download pre-filled, edit the house column, re-upload, blank cells leave that person's house untouched" round trip the student CSV already used, now offered as three side-by-side cards on the Houses index page. Staff is keyed on `staff_id` (the same external identifier the Staff module already uses everywhere). A parent has no standalone deduplicated identifier to key on (a `StudentGuardian` row is per student, a parent with two children at the school has two separate rows), so the parent CSV is keyed on the pair of the child's admission number and the parent's name, matching one export row exactly.
- Issues/Bugs found: one live bug caught by the new tests before shipping, not a real-account defect: `addSponsor()` accessed `$data['student_guardian_id']`/`$data['name']` with plain array access after `$request->validate()`, which omits a nullable key entirely from the returned array when it was not present in the request at all (as opposed to present-but-empty), throwing an `ErrorException` ("Undefined array key") instead of the intended 422. Fixed by reading both through `??` null coalescing, and applied the same defensive fix to the sibling `updateLeadership()`/`assignStaff()`/`assignParent()` actions for consistency even though their real HTML forms always submit every field.
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: 15 new tests in `HouseScreenTest` (house master and assistant both settable; a sponsor can be an existing parent; a sponsor can be a free typed name; adding a sponsor with neither is rejected with 422; a sponsor can be removed; the house page shows the right count and names for all three member groups; a staff member's house can be moved; a parent's house can be moved; distribute places unassigned students as well as staff/parents, skips retired houses, and leaves already placed members alone; distribute refuses to run with zero active houses; the staff CSV template is pre-filled and an upload assigns a house; the parent CSV template is pre-filled and an upload assigns a house; a teacher without `students.manage_settings` cannot set leadership). All 32 House tests passing. Full suite run in the background during this pass. `php artisan tenants:migrate` run and confirmed against every real school for the two new migrations before the distribute pass ran.
- Not built in this pass: a way to bulk-move many staff/parents at once with a checkbox (today it is one inline select per row, the CSV round trip, or the school-wide "distribute" action), and no dedicated "House Sponsor" concept beyond the name/parent-link recorded here (no contact details, no separate sponsor profile page), both left as-is since neither was explicitly asked for.

### Populate every real student with live scores and comments, across two sessions (2026-07-12, extended 2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-12
- Notes: the explicit request left open at the end of the report card workflow build: "develop everything needed for the report card, the grading system and also populate each student with live scores, comments etc (leave some for us to review the workflow, approval etc)", extended the next day: "the real scores should be for all the terms as i want two sessions per school." New `php artisan schools:populate-scores {school?}` command.
  1. **Scores and remarks**: for every class arm's students and every subject that class offers, generates one score per assessment component (respecting each component's real `max_score`), computed from a per (student, term) "ability" figure so a student's spread across subjects reads as one coherent person (a strong student scores consistently higher across their subjects, not pure per-cell noise), with a small per-subject and per-term variance so it is not flatly identical everywhere either. Deterministic per run via `mt_srand(studentId*1000+termId)`, not persisted, just enough consistency within one run for the numbers to look authored rather than random. Every subject's total then goes through the existing `CommentBank::suggestionFor()` (built the same day as part of the report card workflow) for a `SubjectRemark`, and each student's cross-subject average goes through it again for `StudentTermRemark.class_teacher_remark`.
  2. **Two sessions per school** (the 2026-07-13 extension): a school found with only one `AcademicSession` gets a second one added automatically, the year immediately before its existing one (e.g. `greenfield` was on `2025/2026 Session` only, gained `2024/2025 Session`), with its own three terms, using the exact same session+term creation shape `AcademicsController::createSessionWithTerms()` already uses. The school's actual current session/term is never changed, only a second one is added alongside it. Every term across both sessions then gets scores, not just the current one, so the report card's existing cumulative-previous-terms view has real history to actually show, not just one lone term.
  3. **Never touches the review workflow**: the command only ever writes to `AcademicSession`, `Term`, `Score`, `SubjectRemark`, and `StudentTermRemark.class_teacher_remark`, `ReportCardReview` is never read from or written to at all, confirmed by a dedicated test and by re-checking every real school's `ReportCardReview` row count was unchanged by the run (`greenfield`'s 21 pre-existing rows, all `pending_review`, left over from the report card workflow's own build/QA on 2026-07-11, untouched and unrelated to this command).
  4. **Idempotent, never overwrites real data**: a (student, subject, term) with any existing `Score` row is skipped entirely, not topped up component by component, so a real teacher's already-entered marks (`greenfield` had exactly 3 real scores from an earlier live smoke test in this session) are never touched. Same "only if blank" rule for `SubjectRemark` and `StudentTermRemark.class_teacher_remark`. Running the command again is a safe no-op everywhere it already ran.
  5. **Real school prerequisites filled in first, data only, matching the "Grading scales populated for real" precedent**: `qa-academy`, `test`, `test2`, `skultest`, and `data-s` had no assessment structure configured at all (0 `AssessmentComponent` rows), so the command would have skipped every one of them; loaded `config('skulos.default_assessment_components')` onto each via the existing `AssessmentController::applyDefaults()` logic (only ever loads onto an empty structure, never overwrites) before running the population command.
- Issues/Bugs found: none, a net new data-population pass, not a defect fix.
- Resolution: n/a.
- QA: 7 tests in a new `PopulateStudentScoresTest` (scores stay within each component's max and a subject remark is suggested; a subject with any real score is never topped up; running the command twice does not duplicate; `ReportCardReview` is never created or changed; a school with no terms at all is skipped with a clear message; a school with only one session gains a second and every term across both gets scores; a school with no assessment components is skipped with a clear message). Full suite: 439 passed, 0 failed. Run live against all 7 real provisioned schools in two passes (current term only, then re-run after the two-session extension landed): every school now has 2 sessions and 6 terms; `qa-academy`/`test2`/`skultest`/`data-s` each got 65,700 scores/21,900 subject remarks, `test` (the largest, 375 students) got 82,125/27,375, `greenfield` 69,000/23,000 (skipping its 3 pre-existing real ones), `training` correctly wrote 0 of everything since it has no students yet. Spot checked one real student on `greenfield` (Chinedu Garba): 8 subjects with plausible, non-identical totals (45 to 65) and a coherent term remark, and confirmed the pre-existing 3 real scores (student id 659, timestamped from this session's earlier live smoke test) were byte-for-byte unchanged after the run.
- Not built in this pass: nothing further requested here; the report card review/approval workflow itself remains deliberately untouched and ready for the user's own manual testing, per the explicit instruction.

### User profile page with a school-admin approval workflow (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: a long-queued item from the earlier teacher-scoping build's "still queued" list: "a user profile page (click name to view/edit, set preferences) gated behind a school-admin approval workflow before any change takes effect." Genuinely greenfield, confirmed via research first: no profile route/controller/view existed anywhere, the user's name in the sidebar footer (`layouts/app.blade.php`'s `.side-foot`) was plain text with no link, and no generic "pending change awaiting approval" pattern existed to reuse.
  1. **Who needs approval**: rather than invent a new permission or a "is this person an admin" flag, self-edits are gated on the existing `staff.update` permission — a member already trusted to edit a *colleague's* staff record (an owner or admin) is reasonably trusted to edit their own name/email/phone without a second sign-off and it applies immediately; everyone else's edit (class/subject teachers, guardians) is held.
  2. **Schema**: new tenant table `profile_change_requests` (`user_id`, `original` json, `requested` json — only the fields that actually changed, not every field, so the approval screen's diff is clean — `status`, `reviewed_by_user_id`, `reviewed_at`, `notes`) and model `ProfileChangeRequest`, `applicant()`/`reviewer()` built manually (not a plain `belongsTo()`) since `User` is a platform model and a bare relation would inherit the tenant connection and throw "no such table: users", the same fix already applied to `Staff::user()`/`StudentGuardian::user()`.
  3. **Controller**: new `App\Http\Controllers\School\ProfileController`, routes under `school.profile.*` inside the existing tenant-domain `auth`+`school_member` group. `edit()`/`update()` for the member's own page; `update()` diffs submitted values against the live `User` row, applies immediately if `staff.update`, otherwise supersedes any existing pending request for that user (never lets two pile up) and creates a new one. `approvals()`/`approve()`/`reject()` for the admin inbox, gated on the same `staff.update` permission. `approve()` re-checks the requested email/phone are still unique platform wide at approval time (not just at submission time), since time may have passed since the request was filed.
  4. **UI**: the sidebar's user name in `layouts/app.blade.php` is now a real link to the profile page (previously plain text), with a second small "Profile approvals" link shown only to whoever holds `staff.update`. New `school/profile/edit.blade.php` (the form, plus a banner showing any of the user's own pending request's before/after values) and `school/profile/approvals.blade.php` (the admin inbox, approve/reject per row, reject requires a typed reason via the same prompt-based pattern the report card workflow's "send back" already uses).
  5. **Deliberately deferred, explained on the controller itself**: password change is left out of this pass entirely, not folded into the approval gate. No self-service "change my password" flow exists anywhere yet (only an admin resetting someone else's), and gating a user's own password behind another person's approval would be a real security anti-pattern, a compromised account needs to be fixable immediately, not left waiting on someone else's sign-off. "Set preferences" from the original request was also left out, nothing in the product currently defines what a user "preference" would be beyond identity fields (the next queued item, an admin-configurable landing page per role, is a school-wide default, not a personal preference, so it was kept as its own separate item rather than folded in here).
- Issues/Bugs found: one live bug caught by the new tests before shipping: `approve()`'s uniqueness re-check built a closure `where()` with no conditions added when a request only changed `name` (neither email nor phone), which produced an always-true empty conditional group and made every approval falsely report a conflict. Fixed by only running the uniqueness check at all when the request actually touches email or phone.
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: 10 new tests in `ProfileTest` (an owner can view their own profile; an owner's own edit applies immediately and creates no request; a teacher's edit is held and the name does not change until approved; submitting with no actual changes creates nothing; a second edit while one is pending supersedes the first rather than stacking; an admin can approve and it applies; an admin can reject with a reason and the change never applies; rejecting without a reason fails validation; a teacher without `staff.update` cannot open the approvals inbox; email uniqueness is enforced platform wide). Full suite: 449 passed, 0 failed. `php artisan tenants:migrate` run and confirmed against every real school. Live sanity check on the real `greenfield` school via tinker: a real subject teacher (Chidinma Akintola) does not hold `staff.update` (correctly approval-gated), the real school owner does (correctly self-applies).
- Not built in this pass: password self-change and personal "preferences," both explained above; a platform-admin-level `/profile` (this pass is school-portal scoped only, matching how the original request was framed around a school's own staff/parents, a platform super admin's own identity has no natural "admin above them" to approve it anyway).

### Admin-configurable landing page per role, with a platform-wide central override (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: user request: "Admin-configurable landing page per staff/student/parent (default Dashboard)", extended: "all the changes you added should also reflect for the platform owner too who also have a central override for all the schools." Login redirect was previously hardcoded to `school.dashboard` for every single member regardless of role, confirmed via research before building (`LoginController::login()` had zero per-role branching).
  1. **Catalog**: new `config('skulos.landing_page_options')`, a curated picklist of parameterless school routes (Dashboard, Students, Staff, Houses, Classes and Subjects, Results Entry, Grading, Report Cards, Pending Report Card Reviews, Notifications, My Profile), each carrying the permission code needed to actually reach it (or `null` for pages every signed-in member can always reach).
  2. **Two levels, same precedence shape as SMTP settings**: a school's own tenant `Setting` ("landing_pages", a role slug to route name map) wins if set; otherwise the platform owner's central `PlatformSetting` (same key) applies to every school; otherwise Dashboard. `LandingPageController::landingRouteFor(?Role $role): string` (a static helper, callable from `LoginController` without needing a request/response) implements this precedence, plus a safety net: if the resolved route's permission is one the role does not actually hold (a stale or wrong admin choice), it quietly falls back to Dashboard instead of 403ing someone the moment they sign in.
  3. **Two settings screens**: `School\LandingPageController` (`school.settings.landing-pages.*`, gated on `school_info.view`/`school_info.manage_settings`, the same tier as Branding/Mail Settings/Backups) shows the *effective* current value per role (platform default merged underneath the school's own picks, so an admin sees what actually applies right now, not a blank always-Dashboard list) and a new "Landing Pages" link in the school nav's Settings group. `Admin\LandingPageSettingsController` (`admin.settings.landing-pages.*`) is the platform owner's central default editor, same table-of-roles UI, new "Landing Pages" link in the platform nav's Settings group, directly beside Mail Settings, mirroring `MailSettingsController`'s existing platform-default/school-override split exactly.
  4. **Login wiring**: `LoginController::login()` now resolves `$user->roleIn($school)` (null for an unimpersonated platform super admin, who has no role in any one school) and redirects via `LandingPageController::landingRouteFor()` instead of a hardcoded route name.
- Issues/Bugs found: none, both settings layers and the precedence logic worked as designed on the first test run.
- Resolution: n/a.
- QA: 8 new tests in `LandingPageTest` (a school owner can set the school's own choice; a subject teacher actually lands there after a real login POST; the platform default applies when the school has not set its own; a school's own choice overrides the platform default; nothing configured anywhere still lands on Dashboard; a role without the destination page's permission falls back to Dashboard instead of erroring; a platform super admin can set the central default; a teacher without `school_info.manage_settings` cannot change a school's landing pages). Existing `PlatformFlowTest` (26 tests covering login/dashboard flows) re-run and confirmed unaffected, every existing account without a configured landing page still lands on Dashboard exactly as before. No new migration needed, `Setting`/`PlatformSetting` are both pre-existing generic key-value tables.
- Not built in this pass: nothing further requested; every candidate landing page is a route needing no parameter beyond `{school}`, a page needing extra route parameters (e.g. a specific class or student) was deliberately left out of the picklist rather than half-supported.

### Full Student and Parent Portal (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: user directive: "BUILD THE FULL STUDENT AND PARENT PORTAL." Researched first: both roles were "PARTIAL" per section 14 (a dashboard already rendered once an account existed and was linked, but nothing could actually create that account for real, and nothing scoped what either role could see beyond the dashboard, `parent`/`student` held identical minimal grants — `calendar.view`, `communication.view`, `timetable.view` — and no data-level scoping existed for either role at all, so granting them anything broader like `students.view` unscoped would have leaked the whole school).
  1. **Student's own login, mirroring the guardian pattern exactly**: `Student.user_id` existed as a column with nothing upstream ever writing to it. New `createStudentLogin()`/`resetStudentLoginPassword()`/`toggleStudentLoginAccess()` on `StudentController`, byte-for-byte the same shape as the existing `createGuardianLogin()`/etc, an email always collected at creation time (student email is optional, same handling as the guardian flow), backfilling the student's own email if blank. New `Student::user()` relation, built manually (not a plain `belongsTo()`) for the same cross-connection reason `Staff::user()`/`StudentGuardian::user()` already are. New "Student Portal login" card on the student profile page, gated behind `canManage` exactly like the existing Guardians login panel.
  2. **New `App\Services\Rbac\FamilyScope`**, the data-level restriction TeacherScope already established but for family relationships instead of class arms: `studentIds(User, School): ?Collection` returns null for unrestricted roles, `[own student id]` for `student`, or every linked child's id (via `StudentGuardian.guardian_user_id`) for `parent`. Kept as its own service rather than folded into TeacherScope since neither role has a Staff record for TeacherScope's class-arm logic to key off.
  3. **Permission grants**: `parent` and `student` role templates now both hold `students.view` too (previously only calendar/communication/timetable), but it is real and safe specifically *because* FamilyScope narrows what it actually resolves to, never the whole school. `StudentController::index()` gets a new top branch (checked before the existing subject-teacher/scoped-teacher/admin branches): a student with exactly one linked record is redirected straight to their own profile, a parent sees a small "My Children" card list of just their linked children. `show()` gets the same 404-not-403 treatment TeacherScope's class-arm scoping already uses, for the family case.
  4. **The existing student profile page turned out to already be the whole "view my report card" feature**: `StudentController::show()` already renders a full per-subject total/grade/remark breakdown via the already-built `ResultService::studentReport()`, gated only on `students.view`. Once family-scoped `students.view` access was safe to grant, no new "report card viewer" screen was needed at all, the existing page already does the job.
  5. **Nav restriction extended from teacher-scoped roles to family-scoped roles too**: new `config('skulos.family_scoped_roles')` = `['parent', 'student']`, and `nav-school.blade.php`'s existing `$isTeacherScoped`-gated logic (7 call sites) now runs off a new `$isRestrictedNav = $isTeacherScoped || $isFamilyScoped` instead, reusing the exact same `nav_permission_map`/`$alwaysHiddenForScoped` machinery already proven for class/subject teachers rather than building a second parallel nav system. A family-scoped role additionally gets one extra Main-group link, "My Children" (parent) or "My Report" (student), pointed at the same Students route FamilyScope already narrows for them.
  6. **Dashboards made honest**: `RoleDashboard::parent()`'s "Fees Due"/"Messages" tiles showed a hardcoded `0`, which reads as "nothing owed"/"no messages" rather than "not built yet" (standing no-dummy-data rule) — removed entirely rather than faked, replaced with a real "Upcoming Events" figure wired to the existing `CalendarEvent::upcoming()` scope. `RoleDashboard::student()`'s panel now carries the student's own id so the dashboard can link straight to their real report instead of a static "will appear here once published" placeholder. `role-panel.blade.php`'s `children`/`own_result` cases both gained real "View report" buttons.
  7. **RBAC resync**: reseeded the shared role templates (`RbacSeeder`), then additively `syncWithoutDetaching()`'d the new `students.view` permission onto every already-cloned `parent`/`student` role on every real school (only one needed it, `test`'s `parent` role, most real schools had never cloned either role yet since no student/parent portal existed to use it).
- Issues/Bugs found: one, caught immediately by the new tests: `StudentController::index()`'s return type was declared as `\Illuminate\View\View` only, but the new single-student redirect branch returns a `RedirectResponse`, a real 500 on every student login until widened to a union return type.
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: 9 new tests in `StudentParentPortalTest` (admin can create a student's login; a student with one linked record is redirected straight to their own profile; a student can view their own report but a different student's page 404s, not 403s; a parent sees only their linked children and 404s on anyone else's; a parent with no linked children yet sees a helpful message, not an error; a parent never sees admin-only nav items but does see "My Children"; a parent cannot edit their own child's record, `students.update` was never granted; a teacher without `students.update` cannot create a student login; an admin can reset/toggle a student's login access). One existing test (`StudentScreenTest::test_students_index_requires_permission`) updated, it had used `parent` as an example of a permission-less role, which stopped being true the moment `parent` legitimately gained scoped `students.view`, swapped to `security` (genuinely zero grants). Full suite: 466 passed, 0 failed. Live verified against a real account on the `test` school: guardian "Ngozi Okafor" (role `parent`) correctly resolves to her 3 real linked children (siblings sharing one guardian, from the earlier demo-data seeding), confirmed via `FamilyScope::studentIds()` directly against the real tenant database.
- Not built in this pass: the login page itself (role picker, admission-number as a login identifier), self-service/forced password changes, and an admin-approved forgot-password flow are all queued as a following, explicitly separate build, see the next entry.

### Live bug: Grading page listed scales alphabetically, not in pedagogical order (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: user report, live: "the 'Grading' page did not follow the order of how the classes should follow as I mentioned backed then - fix and do audit of other too and take note for future looks like this." `GradingController::index()` was `GradingScale::with('bands')->orderBy('name')`, alphabetical, so "Junior Secondary Scale" sorted before "Nursery and Primary Scale" — the exact same class of bug already fixed for Section/SchoolClass/Subject ordering earlier the same week (creation-order/alphabetical instead of a real `sequence` column driven by the config catalog's declared order).
- Issues/Bugs found: the one described above. A full audit sweep (grep across every Controller/Model for `orderBy('name')`/`sortBy('name')`-shaped queries, cross-checked against config-catalog-declared "real" orders) found no other unfixed instances, every other alphabetical sort in the app is either genuinely arbitrary data with no natural order (houses, staff/student name lists, geography) or was already fixed in the earlier Class Order & Structure pass (Sections, SchoolClasses, Subjects, AssessmentComponents, `ClassArm::orderKey()`'s callers).
- Resolution: new `sequence` column on `grading_scales` (migration `2026_07_13_000004_...`), set from `config('skulos.default_grading_scales')`'s declared array order (Nursery/Primary = 1, Junior = 2, Senior = 3) in `GradingController::applyDefaults()`, only ever filling a still-unset (0) value so a re-run never resets a scale an admin manually reordered, matching the same convention `AcademicDefaults::applySubjectCatalog()` already established. `GradingController::index()` now orders by `sequence` first. `GradingScale::bands()` relation also given an explicit `orderByDesc('min_score')` (highest grade first, A before F) rather than relying on insertion order, closing a latent version of the same risk even though nothing had actually surfaced it yet. Retroactively repaired on all 6 real schools with grading scales loaded (3 scales corrected on each).
- QA: 1 new regression test in `GradingScreenTest` (loading the defaults produces scales in the exact order Nursery and Primary, Junior Secondary, Senior Secondary, not alphabetical). All 8 `GradingScreenTest` tests passing. Live verified against the real `greenfield` school's tenant database directly: `GradingScale::orderBy('sequence')` now reads Nursery and Primary (1), Junior Secondary (2), Senior Secondary WAEC (3).
- Not built in this pass: a manual reorder UI for grading scales (mirroring the "Position" field Sections/Classes/Subjects gained in the earlier ordering fix) — not requested here and a school realistically only ever has 2-3 scales, low value versus the class/subject/section case which could have a dozen+ rows; revisit if a school ever asks to reorder a custom scale.

### Login role picker, admission-number login, forced first-login password change, self-service password change, and dual forgot-password paths (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: user directive, combined multi-part request: "for students, they have option of using admission number to login too along with email and phone number and the default password is same as that of staff and parents. Maybe we need to change how the login page works now so that it ask if you are login in as a staff, parents or learner before one see the needed username and password. Let's add a new feature to all users profile, where they can change their own password if they want too. also, all first time users must change the default password when they login in for the first time. Also let's add a forgot password option to the login page which users can reset their password to a new password they want which the school admin will have to approve and thats when they can use the new one they want." Follow-up clarification split forgot-password into two explicit options: "1. the user enter a new password they want which the admin will have to approve... or 2. the user make use of password reset link and get it as a mail." Asked via AskUserQuestion how to handle the login-form ambiguity (a bare identifier field cannot tell an admission number from a phone number apart from email); user picked "Add a role picker first."
  1. **Role picker on the school login page**: `login-form.blade.php` now shows three role buttons (Staff, Parent, Student) before any identifier/password field, reading `request('as')`. Once a role is chosen, the identifier field's label adapts ("Admission Number, Email, or Phone" for student, "Email or Phone Number" otherwise) and an `as` hidden field carries the choice through submission.
  2. **`LoginController::resolveUser()`**: email is always tried first (unambiguous, contains "@"), admission-number lookup (`Student::where('admission_number', ...)->user_id`) only runs when `as=student`, phone lookup is the fallback otherwise. `as` is nullable in validation, so any existing non-role-picker caller behaves exactly as before.
  3. **Forced first-login password change**: new `must_change_password` boolean on the platform `users` table (excluded from `$fillable`, same pattern as `is_super_admin`), set true by `AccountProvisioningService::provision()`/`resetPassword()` and by `SchoolAdminController::attachAdmin()`. New `RequirePasswordChange` middleware (alias `require_password_change`, added to the authenticated school route group) redirects anywhere else to `school.password.change` until cleared, except a support-impersonating platform admin is never forced through the target account's own gate (checks `session('impersonator_id')`).
  4. **Self-service password change**: `ProfileController::changePasswordForm()`/`updatePassword()`, shared by both the mandatory gate and a new "Change password" link on the profile page, requires the current password, applies immediately, clears `must_change_password`.
  5. **Forgot password, option 1 (admin-approved)**: new tenant table `password_reset_requests` (`App\Models\Tenant\PasswordResetRequest`, `password_hash` stored pre-hashed and `$hidden`). An unauthenticated user picks their own new password; their old password keeps working until a school admin holding `staff.update` approves it from a new card on the Approvals inbox; approval writes the hash directly via `DB::table('users')->update()` (bypassing Eloquent's `hashed` cast to avoid double-hashing) and now also emails the user via a new `PasswordResetApprovedMail`.
  6. **Forgot password, option 2 (email reset link)**: deliberately bypasses Laravel's full Password broker (that pipeline assumes one non-tenant `password.reset` route, and this app's link has to land back on the correct school subdomain) and instead reads/writes Laravel's existing `password_reset_tokens` table directly via `DB::table()`. A random 64-character token is generated, hashed and stored, the raw token goes out in the email (new `PasswordResetLinkMail`) as a query param on a `reset-token.blade.php` form, a 60-minute expiry is enforced, and on submission the password applies immediately (no approval needed, proving inbox access is itself the identity check), clearing `must_change_password` and deleting the token row.
  7. **`forgot-password.blade.php`** now presents both paths side by side, clearly labeled "Option 1: pick your own password" and "Option 2: email me a reset link," so a user is never left guessing which one they used.
- Issues/Bugs found: none in the feature logic itself; three existing tests broke as a direct, expected consequence of the new forced-first-login redirect and were updated rather than reflecting real bugs: `PlatformFlowTest`'s login-page-render assertion (switched from checking literal "Sign in" text, which turned out to also appear elsewhere in the page shell, to checking for the actual form field markup), `PlatformFlowTest`'s created-admin-sign-in test (now expects a redirect to `school.password.change`, not straight to the dashboard, since a freshly attached admin correctly has `must_change_password = true`), and `ParentPortalTest`'s dashboard-content test (added `forceFill(['must_change_password' => false])` since that test's actual intent, dashboard content, was unrelated to the new gate).
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: 13 new tests in `PasswordManagementTest` (admission-number login with and without `as=student`; a newly-provisioned login is forced to change its password; `RequirePasswordChange` blocks other pages but not the change route itself; correct and incorrect current-password change attempts; a forgot-password submission holds the request without changing the password yet; admin approve and reject of a reset request, including asserting the approval email is actually sent; a generic no-account-enumeration message on an unknown identifier; the full email-link flow end to end, extracting the real token from the faked mail and confirming the token row is deleted after use; an expired token and a wrong token are both rejected). Full suite re-run after fixing the 3 incidental breakages above: 481 passed, 0 failed. `php artisan tenants:migrate` confirmed up to date (the one new tenant migration, `password_reset_requests`, had already applied). Live-verified against the real running dev server, not just the test database: `curl -H "Host: greenfield.localhost:8000" http://localhost:8000/login` returns the three role-picker buttons (Staff, Parent, Student) on a real school.
- Not built in this pass: nothing further requested; a rate limit already exists on both forgot-password routes (5 attempts/minute) reusing the same throttle pattern as the trial-request form.

### No-hardcoding audit (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: user directive: "No-hardcoding audit done now and not deferred." Ran a dedicated research pass (Explore agent) across the whole backend against the standing rule (CLAUDE.md: nothing a school admin or the Platform Super Admin could plausibly want to change should be a literal in code, and a config array is only ever a seed/default catalog, never the live runtime source of truth once a DB table exists for it). Full findings list is in the agent's report; this entry covers what was actually fixed versus knowingly left, and why.
  1. **Confirmed already correct, not touched**: `Plan` pricing/features/modules (admin editable from `Admin\BillingController`), `GradingScale`/`GradingBand`, the `Comment` bank, `AcademicDefaults`, `CalendarService::seedDefaults()`, `HouseController::applyDefaults()`, `AssessmentController::applyDefaults()`, `DashboardPreferences`, `IdleTimeoutService`, `NotificationService::emailAudiences()` all already follow the sanctioned "config seeds a DB table, runtime always reads the table" pattern.
  2. **Fixed: platform support email/phone**, shown on the login page and in every transactional email footer, was `config('skulos.support.*')` (itself just `env()`) read directly in 4 view files, no admin override existed at all. New `App\Services\Platform\SupportContactService` (`PlatformSetting` override wins, config/env stays the fallback, same shape as `IdleTimeoutService`), new `Admin\SupportContactController` (`admin.settings.support-contact.*`), new "Support Contact" link in the platform Settings nav group beside Mail Settings/Idle Timeout/Retention. All 4 views (`emails/layout.blade.php`, `emails/plain.blade.php`, `auth/login.blade.php`, `auth/partials/powered.blade.php`) now resolve the service instead of reading config directly.
  3. **Fixed: optional modules picker read `config('skulos.modules')` live** in `Admin\BillingController::optionalModules()` and `Admin\SchoolAdminController::show()`, even though a seeded `modules` DB table already exists (`RbacSeeder::syncModules()`) specifically so this kind of read should never hit config directly, a textbook instance of the exact anti-pattern CLAUDE.md names by name. New `Module::optional(): array` (a static helper on the model, `Module::where('is_core', false)->get()` in the shape both Billing/School Admin screens already expect) replaces both call sites.
  4. **Fixed: duplicated "leadership tier" role slug list**, hardcoded independently in `PlatformDashboardController::index()` (drove the real "how can i have 449 users and 447 administrators" incident from 2026-07-12, the exact class of bug a single source of truth prevents) and `RoleDashboard::forRole()` (a *different*, deliberately narrower set that excludes `school-admin`, since that role gets its own operational dashboard). These are genuinely two different sets, not one duplicated literal, so they were not force merged into one list, which would have reintroduced the original incident by including School Admin in the wrong count. New single source `config('skulos.leadership_role_slugs')` (the narrower, `RoleDashboard`-shaped set) with `PlatformDashboardController` explicitly adding `'school-admin'` back on top for its "total administrators" count, so the relationship between the two is now visible in one place instead of two independently maintained arrays that can silently drift apart again.
  5. **Knowingly left as is, with reasoning**: `plan_feature_catalog`/`add_on_catalog` (config('skulos.php') catalogs read live by `BillingController`, `SchoolAdminController`, `SchoolAddOn::catalogEntry()`) have no DB table at all, unlike `Plan` pricing itself which is correctly DB-backed. Building this out means a new migration, model, seeder, and full CRUD admin screen, a real feature in its own right, not a quick audit fix, so it was not attempted in this pass. Flagged as its own backlog item below rather than silently dropped. `CommunicationService::channelEnabled()` for SMS/WhatsApp/push reads config only with no DB override, but no SMS/WhatsApp/push provider is actually wired up anywhere in the app (no provider integration exists at all yet, this tracker's own "not built" notes confirm it), so there is nothing real for an admin toggle to control yet; building the toggle before the channel itself exists would be a fake control. Revisit both together when SMS/WhatsApp is actually built.
- Issues/Bugs found: none introduced; item 4 above surfaced that the two role lists were never actually identical (the audit agent's first pass assumed they were duplicates of each other, closer reading of `RoleDashboard`'s own code comment confirmed the School Admin exclusion there is deliberate), corrected before merging them to avoid reintroducing the 2026-07-12 incident under a different name.
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: new `NoHardcodingAuditTest` (5 tests): config fallback applies with nothing configured, a platform admin can change the support contact, the login page reflects the change, a non-super-admin cannot reach the settings screen, and `Module::optional()` proven to read the DB (not config) by renaming a module row directly and confirming the renamed value comes back. Also re-ran every test file touching the changed call sites (`RoleDashboardTest`, `ParentPortalTest`, `BillingTest`, `PlatformFlowTest`, `RbacTest`, `SchoolAdminManagementTest`, 78 tests) before the full suite. Full suite: 486 passed, 0 failed.
- Not built in this pass: `plan_feature_catalog`/`add_on_catalog` DB backing (item 5 above, added to the backlog as its own entry, needs a real migration + CRUD screen), the SMS/WhatsApp/push channel toggle (blocked on a provider actually existing to toggle).

### Attendance Management screen (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: queued from the "add all partial builds to the to do task" batch. `Attendance` model and `AttendanceService` (`markClass`, `markStaff`, `classSummary`) already existed and were unit tested (`AttendanceTest`), but no controller, route, or view let a school actually mark or view attendance, per the tracker's own prior "PARTIAL" note.
  1. New `App\Http\Controllers\School\AttendanceController`: `index` (class picker plus roster for a date, with a live 4-status summary row), `markClass` (POST to `AttendanceService::markClass`), `staffIndex`/`markStaff` (a separate, simpler staff register). Student attendance is class-arm scoped through `TeacherScope` exactly like `StudentController`: a class teacher/assistant/subject teacher only ever sees and, if they hold `attendance.create`, marks their own class arm(s), 404 (not 403) on a class arm outside their scope, and a stray/crafted student id submitted outside the class roster is silently dropped rather than acted on.
  2. Staff attendance is gated on `attendance.create` AND `staff.view` together, both already-existing permission codes, so it naturally excludes every teacher-scoped role (none hold `staff.*`) without any new hardcoded role-slug check, purely from the existing permission catalog.
  3. New views `resources/views/school/attendance/index.blade.php` and `.../staff.blade.php`, following the established array-of-inputs-plus-one-submit pattern (`HouseController::assignForm()`/`assignBulk()`): a status `<select>` per row defaulting to that date's existing mark or "present", one "Save register" submit, and a "Mark all present" convenience button (client-side only, still submits the real form).
  4. Wired into the nav: `nav_permission_map['attendance_management'] = 'attendance.view'` and a `builtRoutes` entry so the link renders for every role holding that permission (previously nothing rendered even for a school with the plan feature, since neither the config map nor the built-route existed yet). Added `'attendance'` to `config('skulos.staff_extra_access_modules')` per that array's own comment instruction ("extend this list as more modules get built"), so a staff member can now be individually granted attendance access as an extra override the same way other real modules already work.
- Issues/Bugs found: none in the feature logic; two of the new screen tests needed fixing after first writing them, both test-setup mistakes rather than app bugs: a staff-permission test accidentally created its second user against a different provisioned school than the one it asserted against, and the nav-link test needed `PlanSeeder` seeded and a plan actually assigned to the test school (nothing seeds a default `Plan` automatically in this test suite, unlike production).
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: new `AttendanceScreenTest` (6 tests): an owner can view and mark a class register; a class teacher only sees and can only mark their own class arm, 404 on another; a subject teacher can view but their attempt to mark is a real 403 (they hold `attendance.view` only); a stray student id outside the class roster is silently ignored, not marked; staff attendance requires `staff.view` too, so a class teacher is correctly forbidden from it; the nav link actually renders for a school whose plan includes the feature. Full suite: 492 passed, 0 failed (up from 486, the 6 new tests). No new tenant migration needed, `attendance` table already existed from the original model/service build.
- Not built in this pass: any streak/percentage/date-range trend beyond a single date's summary counts (not in `AttendanceService` today); this is picked back up as part of the new Dashboard Design Cross-Check batch (2026-07-13), which wires this real attendance data into every dashboard card/widget that referenced it across nine different role dashboards.

#### Sub-item: wire real Attendance data into every dashboard that referenced it (2026-07-13)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-13
- Notes: first item of the cross-check batch. `RoleDashboard` gained a shared `attendanceRate(string $personType): ?float` helper (null, not 0%, when nobody has been marked yet today, so an empty register reads as "not marked" rather than "everyone absent", per the standing no-dummy-data rule) and real attendance figures were added to every role dashboard the cross-check flagged:
  - `admin()` (School Owner/Principal/Head Teacher/Vice Principal): added Parents, Subjects, Attendance Rate, Staff Attendance (12 to 16 figures, still a clean 4 column grid).
  - `schoolAdministrator()`: same 4 additions (4 to 8 figures).
  - `classTeam()` (Class Teacher/Class Assistant): added Attendance Today, Present Today, Absent Today, Class Average, for their own class arm only (4 to 8 figures).
  - `subjectTeacher()`: added Attendance Today, Present Today, Absent Today, Classes Marked Today, across every class arm they actually teach (4 to 8 figures).
  - `parent()`: added Attendance Today (aggregate across their linked children) and Report Cards Released this term (2 to 4 figures).
  - `student()`: added Today (their own mark, "Present"/"Absent"/"Not marked"), My Attendance (term rate), Days Present, Days Absent, using the current term's date range (4 to 8 figures).
  - Every addition keeps the `.figs` grid (a fixed `repeat(4,1fr)` on desktop) at a multiple of 4, per the standing design rule that a set of items must resolve to complete rows, not a dangling partial one; where only 1-2 new real metrics existed for a role, a couple of coherent extra ones (e.g. Class Average, Report Cards Released) were added alongside rather than leaving an incomplete row.
- Issues/Bugs found: none in the app logic; the new tests needed two rounds of fixes, both test-authoring mistakes: `round(50.0, 1)` renders as the string `"50"` not `"50.0"` when interpolated (PHP drops the trailing zero on a whole-number float), and the student "Days Present" test needed a real current `AcademicSession`/`Term` created, since the term-scoped attendance query correctly returns nothing when no term exists yet, matching production behavior.
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: 4 new tests in `RoleDashboardTest` (admin dashboard shows real Attendance Rate/Parents/Subjects; a class teacher's dashboard shows their own class's today figures; a student sees their own today/term attendance; a parent sees their children's aggregate attendance today). Full suite: 496 passed, 0 failed (up from 492).

### School Administration live rebrand (Design Lookbook v4) (2026-07-14)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-14
- Notes: user directive after reviewing the design Artifact mockup ("THIS IS BETTER, sHOULD WE CONTINUE OUR TO DO TASKS OR REBUILD ALL OUR CURRENT DASHBOARD LOOKS?", answered with the free-text override "JUST REBUILD THE SCHOOL ADMINSTRATION SO I CAN SEE HOW THE APPLICATION OF THE DESIGN REBRANDING"): applied the approved lookbook artifact's palette/typography/nav rules to the real, live `portal='school'` product UI, not another mockup, scoped so `portal='admin'` (dark navy sidebar, `nav-admin.blade.php`) is completely untouched.
  1. `layouts/app.blade.php`: new `.school-theme` CSS custom-property block (applied to `.shell` only when `portal==='school'`) carrying the lookbook's cream/paper/ink palette (`--bg:#F5EEDC`, `--panel:#FFFDF6` "card", `--shell:#FBF6EA` "paper"), serif numbers (`--font-serif`, Georgia stack, no new font network request) over a sans body, and light/dark-mode variants of both. `School::branding()`'s colour fallback changed from the old `#E8A33D` to the lookbook's `#C88A2E` (single source of truth every school-portal page reads through `$school->branding()['color']`), so an unbranded school's `--accent` genuinely matches the new system by default; a school's own saved brand colour still always overrides it exactly as before. `--accent-ink` stays the existing dynamic `ColorContrast::inkFor()` computation for every case, no hardcoding, it naturally resolves to white for the new default amber (luminance 0.306, below the util's 0.4 threshold) without any special-casing.
  2. `nav-school.blade.php`: swapped the `$link`/`$soonLink` closures' DOM order from icon-then-label to label-then-icon (lookbook nav rule: "sidebar labels LEFT, monogram icons RIGHT", an explicit exception to the project's general no-left-alignment rule), icon pinned right via `margin-left:auto` (only outside the collapsed icon-rail state). `nav-admin.blade.php` is a separate file, untouched.
  3. Dashboard figure cards (`school/dashboard.blade.php`) recentred (standing "card icon + title centred" design rule) with serif numbers; icons in the school theme now render like the lookbook's actual "data-fig" card: quiet, monochrome, a single small outline icon tucked in the corner at low opacity, no per-item colour chip, no accent bar (the platform admin portal's own multi-colour classic figure treatment is untouched, this only overrides under `.school-theme`).
  4. Added a `health` icon glyph to `layouts/partials/icon.blade.php`, wired to the School Health Score fig (was reusing the generic gear/settings icon).
  5. Verification method: no browser/screenshot tool is available in this environment, so verification used (a) the full Blade-rendering test suite across every role, which would fail on any compile error, (b) a disposable `actingAs()` feature test that rendered the real `school.dashboard` route for a freshly provisioned test school and dumped the exact HTML byte-for-byte to a scratch file for direct inspection (not a mockup, the same controller/view/CSS a real login hits), removed after use, and (c) republishing that captured HTML as a Claude Artifact so the user could see the real rendered result directly in a browser.
- Issues/Bugs found and fixed, all from the user's first-round screenshot review of the Artifact snapshot:
  1. Sidebar didn't match the approved lookbook artifact at all: the artifact's actual nav (`.app-nav`) is light paper (`--paper #FBF6EA`), not the dark ink rail I'd shipped first; corrected `--side`/`--side-ink`/`--side-mut`/`--side-active` to light-on-light tokens matching the artifact exactly.
  2. "Add staff" CTA button text was black, not white. Root cause was not the ink logic (verified correct, `ColorContrast::inkFor()` already resolves the new default amber to white) but that `School::branding()['color']` was still hardcoded to the *old* `#E8A33D` (luminance 0.437, correctly dark-inked), so the new `#C88A2E` default I'd set in `app.blade.php` never actually reached a real page, only my synthetic first test happened not to go through the controller. Fixed at the real source (`School::branding()`), not with a special case.
  3. Figure cards didn't match the lookbook's actual "data-fig" style: I had kept the old design's multi-colour icon chip + left accent bar. Rebuilt to the lookbook's quiet, monochrome, corner-icon treatment.
  4. "Too much, let's only have two rows and a button to view more": added a `fig-extra` class (items 9+) hidden by default with a `figsMoreToggle` button (`grid`/`hidden` via `display:revert` so it always resolves to a complete additional set of rows, never a partial one, when expanded).
  5. "THE BOX SIZE ARE NOT SAME": the Notifications/Calendar pair (and the Today's/Upcoming birthdays pair) had `style="align-items:start"` on their `.grid.cols-2` wrapper, so a card with more text content didn't stretch to match its sibling's height. Removed the override on both, grid's own default `stretch` now keeps them equal.
  6. "LACK OF SPACING BETWEEN Announcements... AND Quick actions": `.ann-card` had no `margin-bottom` at all (relied on being inside a flex `banner-row` that isn't used in the default/classic layout), added it, reset to 0 specifically inside `.banner-row` so the vibrant/modern layouts don't get doubled spacing.
  7. Own bug found while implementing item 4, not from user feedback: the Blade shorthand directive `@php($figExtraClass = $i >= 8 ? ' fig-extra' : '')` compiled to invalid, unclosed PHP (`<?php(...)` with no trailing `; ?>`) specifically in the context of this file's full size/complexity (a minimal isolated reproduction of the exact same line compiled correctly, so this is some Blade compiler edge case tied to surrounding content, not a simple typo); switched to the long-form `@php ... @endphp` and it compiled cleanly.
- Resolution: see above.
- QA: full suite run twice (once after the initial rebuild, once after the correction pass), 531 passed / 0 failed both times, no regressions. Did I fully solve what the user asked? Yes for the explicit corrections given; the design Artifact still has more screens (other roles' dashboards, login, marketing) not yet asked to be rebuilt live, and only the dashboard page's own figure-card/layout specifics were reworked, other school-portal pages inherit only the shared shell/nav/palette layer, not a page by page pass. Did I change or break anything not asked? No, `portal='admin'` and every existing test were re-verified untouched.
- Not built in this pass: the remaining `figs-vibrant`/`fig-modern`/`fig-erp` alternate dashboard layout variants were not restyled to the lookbook (only the default `classic` variant, the one shown in every review screenshot, was); no other school-portal screen beyond the dashboard was individually reworked past what the shared layout/nav change gives it for free; login page, other roles' dashboards, and marketing site restyle are still only in the Artifact mockup, not live.

### School Administration live rebrand — second correction pass (2026-07-14)
- Status: Done
- Started: 2026-07-14
- Notes: further screenshot feedback on the corrected Artifact snapshot ("LOOKS BETTER BUT SOME TEXT LOOKS FADED AND I WANT THE WHOLE BACKGROUND CHANGE TO WHITE INSTEAD OF THE CURRENT LIGHT YELLOW WE HAVE ACROSS ALL THE PAGES", "aLSO, LETS INCREASE THE SIZE OF THE ICONS AND MAKE IT BIGGER"). Two changes, both in `.school-theme`'s token block (`layouts/app.blade.php`) and the dashboard's figure-card CSS (`school/dashboard.blade.php`):
  1. `--bg`/`--panel`/`--shell` all moved from the cream/paper tones (`#F5EEDC`/`#FFFDF6`/`#FBF6EA`) to pure white (`#FFFFFF`), in both light and dark-mode variants (dark mode's own near-black tones adjusted proportionally, no longer graduated paper-to-card, since there is no "yellow" to remove there but the same "flatten to one surface tone" logic applies). `--side-active` (the nav row hover/active highlight) could no longer just alias `var(--panel)`, since sidebar and content are now the identical white and that would make the highlight invisible, given its own explicit light cream `#F5EEDC` so an active/hovered nav row still visibly stands out against the now-white sidebar (dark mode equivalent: `#211C17` against the `#171310` sidebar).
  2. `--mut`/`--mut-dim` (the label/caption colour beneath every big serif number, e.g. "STUDENTS", "BOYS") raised from 62%/42% ink opacity to 68%/58%: at .42 opacity the label text was reading as washed out, especially now that the surrounding surface is a brighter white rather than warm cream, so the same low-opacity dark text has less inherent contrast against it.
  3. The figure card's corner icon grew from `1.15rem` to `1.9rem` (svg and its container both), opacity nudged from `.55` to `.6` for legibility at the larger size, with the card's own top padding increased from `2rem` to `2.5rem` so the bigger icon still has clearance above the number and never overlaps it.

- Resolution: n/a.
- QA: full suite re-run after this pass, 531 passed / 0 failed, same as before, no regressions. Verified via the same disposable `actingAs()` snapshot-and-inspect method as the first pass (no browser tool available in this environment); confirmed via grep on the captured HTML that `--bg`/`--panel`/`--shell` all resolve to `#FFFFFF`, `--mut-dim` to the new `.58` opacity, and the corner icon markup to `1.9rem`, then republished the same Artifact URL for the user to check directly in a browser.
