- Status: Done
- Notes: user request "make it possible that the admin can go back to another session and term to generate something needed, add it as a feature which the admin can also pass to other users", answered via AskUserQuestion as "Report Cards for a past term + Student Reports/broadsheet for a past term" (both `school.report-cards.edit` and `school.reports.students.index` already accepted a term parameter once checked, they just needed a real term picker on screen, added to both). User then asked to "add a submenu for term broadsheets too" — `student_reports` (already renamed "Term Broadsheets" in the previous nav-gap-closing entry) nested under Academic > Examinations alongside "Enter Scores" and "Grading", using the same `$academicSubgroups['Examinations']` mechanism the score-entry window build introduced, keyed order fixed via an explicit `foreach (['enter_scores','term_broadsheets','grading'] as ...)` loop rather than catalog iteration order. Also built **Custom Report Builder** (Advance, previously "NOT BUILT" in the feature catalog): a new `report_builder_sources` config catalog (students/staff, each with label/permission/columns) drives a generic `ReportBuilderController` — pick a source, pick columns, export CSV — reusing `TeacherScope` so a scoped teacher's student export is silently limited to their own students, never a bypass around the same restriction the rest of the app enforces.
- QA: `PlanFeatureNavTest` cases for the nested Examinations ordering and the Custom Report Builder nav link rendering as real (not "Soon"); `ReportBuilderTest` (6 tests: source listing, CSV export/columns, teacher-scope enforcement, permission gate). Full suite green.

### Declined: hidden/unremovable "backdoor" super admin account for self-hosted deployments (2026-07-18)
- Status: Declined, no code written
- Notes: user asked to make a school's own cPanel-hosted copy of SKULOS possible ("create the school and everything needed then copy it into their cpanel"), specifically requesting "a super admin details which no one can change as it will serve as my backend door incase the school try to rip me off". Declined this specific mechanism: an undisclosed, unremovable account contradicts the project's own prior "Support access is audited impersonation, never a backdoor" decision (see CLAUDE.md working conventions) and carries real legal exposure (a hidden privileged account on a customer's own infrastructure). Offered two disclosed alternatives instead; user chose **"Self-hosted with a disclosed license check"**, then specified lockout behaviour as **"Grace period, then full lockout"** — see the Self-Hosted Licensing entry below for what was actually built.

### Platform admin portal parity batch: Backups matrix, Settings hub, Platform Team management, buried nav items surfaced (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user request: "so many features that we have in the school end are missing under the platform owner end, let's replicate and build more as I need full control of the whole thing." An Explore-agent audit of the platform-admin portal against the school portal found: Modules/Backups/Broadcast/Audit Log were only ever reachable via dashboard quick-action links, never in the sidebar at all; the 6 platform settings screens (Mail/Landing Pages/Idle Timeout/Retention/Support Contact/Subscription Bank) had no single hub, each only linkable by a direct URL; and there was no way at all to grant or revoke additional platform super admin accounts short of the `platform:make-super-admin` console command (no visibility into who already had access). Built: `BackupAdminController::index()` — a cross-school backup matrix (latest backup per school, overdue flag computed from `backup_frequency`); `PlatformSettingsController::index()` — a card grid linking every settings screen in one place (`admin.settings.index`, replacing 6 separate sidebar links with one "Platform Settings" entry); `PlatformTeamController` — grant a new platform admin (forces a password change on first login, audited `platform.team.granted`), revoke another's access (blocks self-revoke and blocks a concurrent revoke race from reaching zero admins as defense in depth, audited `platform.team.revoked`); added Modules/Backups/Broadcast/Audit Log/Platform Team to the actual sidebar (`nav-admin.blade.php`) where they belonged all along.
- Issues/bugs found while building: a written "the last remaining admin cannot be revoked" test turned out to be untestable via the HTTP layer alone (the self-revoke check always fires first in the only reachable sequential scenario) — removed rather than keep a misleading test, and the controller's own doc-comment was corrected to say the check is defense-in-depth against a *concurrent* double-revoke, not something a single sequential test can trigger. `admin/settings-index.blade.php`'s `<a class="card">` links needed an explicit inline `display:block`, since `.card` has no `display` rule of its own and an anchor defaults to inline, silently breaking the grid layout.
- QA: `BackupsMatrixTest` (6 tests), `PlatformSettingsHubTest` (2 tests), `PlatformTeamTest` (5 tests). Full suite (~160 admin/platform-scoped tests) re-run clean.

### Marketing Pages editor, linked from the platform admin portal (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user request, same thread as the parity batch above: "also link the platform owner end to the marketing pages so it's easier to make changes to any of the pages." New `MarketingPageController`: a strict allow-list of the 8 real `site/*.html` filenames (never arbitrary file access — `array_key_exists` against the allow-list, not a path built from user input), a plain `<textarea>` raw-HTML source editor (no rich editor, deliberately simple), reads/writes the real files on whatever disk this Laravel process is running on. Disclosed plainly in the UI copy and commit message that this only edits the file on **this server**: in production, cPanel (Laravel) and GitHub Pages (the static `/site` marketing HTML) are genuinely different hosts, so a live save here does not by itself publish to the live marketing site, that still needs a commit and push through the existing `site/**` -> Pages Actions workflow. Locally, `base_path('../site')` does resolve to the real sibling folder, so local edits are immediately visible.
- QA: `MarketingPageEditorTest` (5 tests: index lists real pages, edit shows real HTML source, saving writes the file and is restored via a `finally` block afterward — verified clean via `git status --short site/` showing no stray diff, an unknown page name 404s rather than exposing arbitrary file access, a non-super-admin is blocked). All green.

### Self-hosted licensing: disclosed grace-then-lockout check-in (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: the feature the "declined backdoor" entry above was resolved into, confirmed by the user via two AskUserQuestion answers: "Self-hosted with a disclosed license check", then "Grace period, then full lockout" for what happens when the check fails. Built as `App\Services\Licensing\LicenseService`, playing two roles depending on which side of a self-hosted relationship the deployment is on:
  - **Platform side** (the main SKULOS deployment): `schools` gained `is_self_hosted`/`license_key`/`license_issued_at`/`license_last_verified_at`/`license_grace_started_at`. A new "Self-hosted license" card on the platform admin's School detail page (`admin.schools.show`) lets a platform admin enable self-hosting for a customer (issues a `SKL-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX` key), regenerate the key (invalidates the old one, e.g. after a suspected leak), or revoke self-hosting entirely (that customer's next check-in then fails and its own grace period takes over). A public, throttled `POST /api/license/verify` endpoint (`LicenseVerifyController`) answers an incoming check-in: valid only if the key matches a school flagged `is_self_hosted` AND that school's own platform-side `status` is currently active — so the exact same suspend/reinstate and subscription-demand/reinstate actions already built for billing lockout also naturally cut off (or restore) a self-hosted customer's license the next time it calls home, no separate billing-vs-license logic needed.
  - **Self-hosted side** (a school's own separate deployment, only active when `config('skulos.self_hosted')` — env `SKULOS_SELF_HOSTED` — is true): a new scheduled command `license:check` (hourly, safe to run on every deployment including the main platform's own, since it no-ops instantly unless the env flag is set) calls the platform's verify endpoint with this installation's own configured key. A valid response clears any grace period and reinstates access if it had been locked; an invalid response OR an unreachable endpoint (deliberately treated the same, otherwise a school could simply firewall the check to escape it forever) starts a grace clock the first time it happens, then, once `config('skulos.self_hosted_license.grace_days')` (default 14) elapses without a successful check-in, locks the school out via the exact same `School::STATUS_SUSPENDED` + a new `SUSPENSION_REASON_LICENSE_EXPIRED` that `ResolveTenant` already enforces for billing, just a different disclosed 403 message. While grace is running (before lockout), every school-portal page shows a persistent warning banner (`layouts/app.blade.php`, styled like the existing impersonation bar) naming how many days are left. A new self-hosted-side settings screen (`admin.settings.self-hosted-license`, added to the Settings hub) is where whoever runs the self-hosted install enters the key they were given and sees this installation's own status.
- Nothing hardcoded: grace period length and the verify endpoint URL are both env/config driven (`config('skulos.self_hosted_license')`), never literals in the check logic.
- QA: `SelfHostedLicenseTest` (16 tests: platform admin enable/regenerate/revoke, regenerating a non-self-hosted school 404s, a non-super-admin is blocked, the verify endpoint's valid/unknown-key/suspended-school responses, `checkSelfHosted()` no-ops unless the env flag is set, clears grace and reinstates on a valid response, starts (but does not immediately lock on) an invalid response, treats an unreachable endpoint the same as invalid via `Http::fake()` throwing a `ConnectionException`, locks out once the grace period has actually elapsed, the settings screen shows status and saves a key, and a locked-out school 403s through the real subdomain-routed `ResolveTenant` middleware with the disclosed message text). All 16 passing. Full backend regression suite re-run after this and the three entries above, to catch any interaction between the new `schools` columns/status branch and the pre-existing subscription-suspension paths.
- Deployment runbook added to `DEPLOYMENT.md` (user request: "what do i copy or install in the school cpanel, create an instruction for this"): a new "Self-hosted deployment" section, what to copy (`backend/` only), what the cPanel account needs (PHP 8.2+, a MySQL database/user, Composer via SSH or a pre-built `vendor/`, cron), and the full 9 step sequence from creating the database through confirming the first successful check-in.

### Report Builder: checkbox/label vertical alignment fix (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user report, a screenshot of the Columns checklist with a checkbox visibly floating between two lines of a wrapped label ("First Name", "Date of Birth", etc.) instead of sitting beside its own text. Root cause: `align-items:center` on a flex label centers the checkbox against the whole height of a two-line wrapped label, not its first line, the same shape of bug as the badge/text overlap fix earlier in this session. Fixed to `align-items:flex-start` plus a small `margin-top` on the checkbox to align it with the text's cap height, and widened the grid column minimum slightly for more breathing room (`backend/resources/views/school/reports/builder.blade.php`).

### "Make all the coming soon under Foundation and Advance active" (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done for everything except Timetable Management, which the user confirmed via AskUserQuestion should stay deferred (2026-07-15 decision reconfirmed, not reversed)
- Notes: audited every Advance tier plan feature key against `nav-school.blade.php`'s `$builtRoutes` map to find which ones actually still fell through to a "Soon" nav link. Foundation had zero real gaps (Knowledge Base/Staff Onboarding/Standard Support are the "support" catalog category, deliberately excluded from the nav loop entirely as service commitments, not missing screens). Advance had four: `custom_report_card_designer` (already fully built as the drag-and-drop Layout Designer, just never wired into the catalog, one line fix), `timetable_management` (genuinely unbuilt, confirmed deferred, left as "Soon"), `teacher_workload` (built this session, see below), `academic_insights` (built this session, see below).
- **Academic Insights**: whole school, subject level ranking (average/highest/lowest/students assessed per subject, strongest first) plus a school wide grade distribution, for a picked term, distinct from the Student Performance Dashboard (class/student level, current term only). `ResultService::subjectAveragesForTerm()`/`gradeDistributionForTerm()` (same sum-components-then-normalize approach as the rest of `ResultService`), `AcademicInsightsController`, `school.examinations.academic-insights`. Gated behind `examinations.approve_results` (the same blanket policy tier as the assessment window settings), a scoped teacher's own reduced view would not mean much for a subject-wide-across-the-school ranking.
- **Teacher Workload**: real subjects/classes/students-taught counts per staff member, computed from `TeachingAssignment` rows plus class-teacher/assistant-teacher assignment on `ClassArm`, never a manual entry or a timetable period count (there is no Timetable Management yet). `TeacherWorkloadController`, `school.staff.workload`, gated behind `staff.view`.
- QA: `AcademicInsightsAndWorkloadTest` (4 tests: subject ranking/grade distribution with real seeded scores, permission gate on Academic Insights, real subject/class-teacher counts on Teacher Workload, permission gate there too). All green.

### Report Card Layout Designer rebuilt with a genuine live preview (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user feedback on the drag-and-drop designer built earlier this session: "the report card designer is poorly done, rebuild using the report card templates as idea. also there should be a live preview of how it will look like which is where all the items should also be dragged to or remove" plus "the report card designer should have one already to edit". The original version used three abstract dashed boxes (Header/Info Strip/Unused) with plain text chips, nothing about it resembled an actual report card. Rebuilt so the "Header" and "Info Strip" drop zones ARE the real `.rc-head`/`.rc-id` markup from `report-cards/render.blade.php` (same CSS classes, copied verbatim with a documented, deliberate exception for column count at the designer's narrower width), and every block inside them is rendered through the exact same `_layout-block.blade.php` partial the real report card uses, fed `ReportCardService::sample()`'s existing sample dataset (already used by the Template preview screen, "Ada Grace Obi" etc.), so what an admin drags around IS the real markup, not a lookalike mockup that could drift from it. It already loads pre-populated (`ReportCardLayout::zones()` already fell back to `config('skulos.default_report_card_layout')`, this was true before too but the old visual style obscured it). Interaction: drag blocks between Header/Info Strip/an "Available blocks" tray (desktop), or click/tap a block to select it and use a compact toolbar (Move up/down, Move to the other zone, Remove) plus "+ Header"/"+ Info Strip" quick-add buttons on tray items (touch friendly, no drag required, per the project's standing mobile-first rule). Selecting a block outlines it rather than cluttering the live preview with permanent per-block controls, keeping the preview visually accurate.
- Issues/bugs found while building: the exact documented Blade single-line `@php($expr)` compiler bug recurred (`@php($placed = ...)` followed immediately by another `@php(...)` line with no blank separator silently swallowed everything up to the next `?>` as one malformed PHP blob, later surfacing as "Undefined variable $blocksJson" many lines away) — caught via the same "clear the view cache, read the compiled PHP directly" diagnostic already documented in CLAUDE.md, fixed by switching to the block `@php ... @endphp` form.
- QA: `ReportCardLayoutDesignerTest` (4 tests: the live preview shows real sample data, the designer loads pre populated with the default layout, saving still works after the rebuild, a non report-card-viewer is blocked). All green, plus the full `ReportCard*`/`PlanFeatureNavTest` suite (86 tests) re-run clean to confirm the rebuild and nav changes didn't disturb anything.

### Narrative Evaluation: per-class comment based report cards, every plan (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user request: "add a new settings for all admins across all plan that allows the admin... to set a class or some classes to have the Narrative Evaluation style of report where scores are not entered into the student report card but more of Narrative Evaluation (comment based). add it under examination as a sub menu, the admin select the class it should be. populate all Creche/Reception across the schools with this type of report." A comment based "narrative" report card TEMPLATE and its entry mechanism (`StudentSubjectComment`, the class-entry screen) already existed from the Report Card enrichment build earlier this session, but only as one school wide template choice (`ReportCardPreferences::template()`), an all-or-nothing setting with no way to mix a scored Primary/Secondary with a narrative Creche/Reception. This makes that same choice selectable per class instead.
- Data model: `school_classes.evaluation_style` (`'scores'` default / `'narrative'`), `SchoolClass::isNarrativeEvaluation()`. Deliberately NOT a `plan_feature_catalog` entry (user directive: "for all admins across all plan"), a pedagogical setting every school gets regardless of tier, not a paid feature.
- Settings screen: `NarrativeEvaluationController`, gated behind `examinations.approve_results` (admin only, "only admin can set this"), a single declarative checklist form (every class listed, tick the ones that should be narrative, Save sets ticked classes to narrative and un-ticked ones back to scores in one action) rather than per-row actions, since there is only ever one meaningful action here. Nested under Academic > Examinations as its own submenu (`school.examinations.narrative-evaluation`), reusing the exact same `$academicSubgroups['Examinations']` nested-subsection mechanism Enter Scores/Term Broadsheets/Grading already share, appended unconditionally (not catalog gated) the same way "Report settings" already is for the identical "every plan gets this" reasoning.
- Enforcement, three integration points into existing code, all keyed off `$class->isNarrativeEvaluation()`: (1) `ExaminationController::allowedPairs()` excludes a narrative class from the Enter Scores class/subject picker entirely, in both the blanket-access and per-teacher-assignment branches, satisfying "scores are not entered"; (2) `ReportCardController::classEntry()` forces `$template = 'narrative'` for a narrative class regardless of the school's own global template preference, so the comment entry screen (and its subject list) shows for that class even on a school otherwise using Classic/Modern/etc.; (3) `ReportCardController::show()` (the real per-student render) does the same override, so a narrative class's student always gets the comment based report card layout, never the scored one, independent of the school wide setting.
- Backfill: `php artisan skulos:backfill-narrative-evaluation`, a one-off idempotent command (loops every provisioned school, sets any class matching `%creche%`/`%reception%` to narrative), matching `config('skulos.default_class_structure')`'s built-in "Creche/Reception" class name (one combined class, not two). Run locally against all 8 provisioned dev schools (7 had a matching class; `debugnav` did not). The new `evaluation_style` column also required `php artisan tenants:migrate` immediately, per the standing tenant-migration gotcha, run locally before the backfill.
- QA: `NarrativeEvaluationTest` (7 tests: an admin can set a class to narrative, saving declaratively reverts an unchecked class back to scores, a plain teacher is blocked from the settings screen, a narrative class is excluded from the score entry screen while a scored sibling class still shows, the class-entry screen shows real comment fields for a narrative class even when the school's global template is not narrative, a narrative class's real student report card renders the narrative template even when the school's global template is not, the nav shows the new submenu for an admin). All green.
- Follow-up fix same day (screenshot of the settings table): the checkbox column was the table's last column, and since the other two columns (Class/Section) have short content, the browser's auto table layout gave it a disproportionate share of the row width, visually stranding the checkbox far from its own label. Rebuilt using the project's own established checkbox-first bulk-action pattern (a narrow first column, a header "select all", a read-only "Current style" badge column instead), the same shape as the Houses reactivate/retire screens.

### Report Card Designer: whole-page rebuild, not just the header (2026-07-18)
- Status: Done
- Notes: further user feedback on the same-day Layout Designer rebuild, after seeing it live: "so many blocks are missing, unable to move the logo to left, right or center, everything should be movable, all the scores gridbox are missing, where are the footer blocks like class teacher remark, headteacher/principal, stamp and signature, make it much more holistic than this simple one." The designer had only ever covered the header/identity strip; the subject scores table, subject comments, summary boxes, behavioural traits/physical stats, grade key, remarks, and stamp/signature were all still fixed, unmovable, un-hideable parts of `render.blade.php`.
- Every one of those seven regions was extracted verbatim into its own partial (`resources/views/school/report-cards/_body-{key}.blade.php`, e.g. `_body-subjects_table`, `_body-remarks`, `_body-signoff`), registered in a new `config('skulos.report_card_body_sections')` catalog. `render.blade.php` now renders them through one `@foreach` over an ordered section list rather than a fixed top-to-bottom sequence; `ReportCardLayout::bodyOrder()`/`setBodyOrder()` store which sections show and in what order (Setting-backed, same pattern as the header/info zones), `effectiveBodyOrder(bool $enabled)` falls back to every section in its original order whenever the custom layout is off, so a school never using the designer sees zero behavioural change.
- Header block alignment ("unable to move the logo to left, right or center"): each Header zone block now carries its own `align-self` (left/center/right), stored per block key in `ReportCardLayout::headerAlign()`/`setHeaderAlign()`, since `.rc-head-custom` is a flex column, `align-self` lets one block (the logo) sit left while its siblings stay centered, independent of each other.
- Designer UI: the live preview now shows the whole assembled page (header, info strip, then every enabled body section in order, inside the same `.rc-sheet` card), each body section rendered through its real partial with the same sample data as the header/info blocks so a "Scores Table" or "Remarks" section previews with real-looking content, not a placeholder. A second "Available sections" tray (parallel to the existing "Available blocks" tray) holds hidden sections; drag or "+ Add to page" brings one onto the live preview, drag off (or Remove from the selection toolbar) takes it back off. A selected Header block's toolbar gains a left/center/right pill group.
- Issues/bugs found while building, all the same underlying Blade compiler quirk (documented in CLAUDE.md, "always keep a space/newline between Blade directives") recurring in new shapes: (1) a second single-line `@php($unusedSections = ...)` immediately followed by `@forelse` on the Available Sections tray, same fix as before (block `@php...@endphp` form); (2) a single-line `@php($align = ...)` inside the header block `@foreach` loop, this one's `@foreach` itself silently failed to compile (stayed as literal uncompiled text) because it was immediately followed by the `@php` on the very next line with no blank line, eventually fixed by removing the intermediate `@php` entirely and inlining the alignment expression directly into the `style="align-self:{{ ... }}"` echo; (3) even after fixing every known adjacency, a block `@php...@endphp` computing `$bodySections` right before its own `@foreach` continued to fail unpredictably (its `@php` compiled to literal uncompiled text while its own `@endphp` compiled correctly, even with a blank line separating it from the prior `@endif`, and even after moving its explanatory comment out to a Blade `{{-- --}}` comment) — given the "intermittent" nature CLAUDE.md's own note already flags, the reliable fix was to stop computing this value in the view at all: moved the enabled/disabled fallback logic into `ReportCardLayout::effectiveBodyOrder(bool $enabled)` and pass the final, already-resolved list straight from the controller, so the view only ever does a plain `@foreach` with no adjacent `@php` block to trip over.
- QA: `ReportCardLayoutDesignerTest` grew from 4 to 7 tests (added: the designer shows body sections and the Available Sections panel, saving persists header alignment and body section order, a narrow body order of just one section actually reorders the real report card, verified via `assertDontSee('Total Score')` on a report card whose only enabled section was Stamp & Signature). Full `ReportCard*`/`NarrativeEvaluation*`/`PlanFeatureNav*` suite (101 tests) re-run clean after this and the Narrative Evaluation entry above.

### Timetable Management (Advance plan feature, un-deferred and built 2026-07-19)
- Status: Done
- Notes: previously DEFERRED 2026-07-15, reconfirmed deferred again as recently as this same session (AskUserQuestion: "Leave it deferred") when every other Advance "Soon" item was closed out, then the user directly asked to build it: "work on the timetable management." A genuinely net new module, no model/controller/view existed before this. The RBAC permission shape (`timetable.*` for admin tier roles, `timetable.view` for every scoped role) already existed in `config('skulos.roles')` well before any screen did, so gating fell straight out of what was already there.
- Data model: `timetable_periods` (a school's own period/slot catalog: name, sequence, start/end time, `is_teaching_period` false for Break/Lunch rows shown on the grid but never assignable), seeded from `config('skulos.default_timetable_periods')` via an "Apply defaults" action, the same seed-then-DB-table shape grading_scales/comments/report card traits already use. `timetable_entries` is the actual weekly grid: class arm + day of week (a key into `config('skulos.school_week_days')`, not an integer) + period + subject + staff, unique on (class arm, day, period) so a class can never show two subjects in the same slot.
- The one real piece of domain logic, `TimetableService::saveClassArmWeek()`: the same teacher cannot be double booked into two different classes at the same day and period. A DB unique constraint could not express this (uniqueness is scoped to the class arm, not the teacher), so it is checked before every write; a conflicting cell is silently skipped and reported back in the flash message rather than failing the whole save, the same "skip and report" shape the report card lock check and the assessment window check already established.
- Admin vs everyone else: only an admin tier role (`timetable.update`, granted via the blanket `timetable.*` a vice principal/owner/admin/principal/head teacher already held) can browse and edit any class's grid (`school.timetable.index`/`school.timetable.save`). Every scoped role (`timetable.view` only: teacher, class teacher, subject teacher, class assistant, parent, student) never sees that screen at all, only their own read only "My Timetable" (`school.timetable.mine`) — a teacher's own real teaching slots via their linked Staff row, a student/parent's own linked child's class grid. The nav link itself resolves to a different route depending on which tier the signed in role is, computed once in `nav-school.blade.php` using the same `$isRestrictedNav` flag every other scoped-vs-admin nav decision already uses.
- Issues/bugs found while building: none new, the same "run `tenants:migrate` after adding a tenant migration" standing step was followed (all 8 local dev schools migrated clean).
- QA: `TimetableTest` (7 tests: applying default periods, building a class's grid end to end, a teacher double booked across two classes in the same slot is skipped not double booked, a scoped teacher 403s on the admin grid editor, a teacher sees their own schedule on My Timetable, a student sees their class's schedule on My Timetable, the nav renders correctly). Plus a `PlanFeatureNavTest` case. All green.

### Global checkbox sizing bug found and fixed at the source (2026-07-19)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user report on the Notifications page (screenshot: a huge blank area with a tiny checkbox floating in it, "Subscription payment verified" text squeezed into a narrow far-right column) — "too many formatting errors, the text for each notification should be seen clearly and that of be checkbox should be reduced to the left side and very small cell." Root cause traced to `layouts/app.blade.php`'s own global `input,textarea,select{width:100%;padding:.65rem .8rem;...}` rule (written for text-style inputs), which was silently also applying to every plain `<input type="checkbox">` in the entire app, stretching each one into a large, heavily padded box with the tiny native glyph floating somewhere inside it. Fixed at the source: excluded `[type="checkbox"]`/`[type="radio"]` from that rule and gave them their own small, native-sized rule (`1.05rem` square, `accent-color:var(--accent)` for on-brand theming). This retroactively fixes every checkbox across the whole app that never had an explicit inline-style override, not just Notifications, including several built earlier this same session (Narrative Evaluation's select-all, Report Builder's column list, Timetable's period form, the Report Card Designer's "enabled" toggle).
- QA: visually confirmed on Notifications; every existing bulk-action/checkbox-driven feature test (Houses, Notifications, Report Builder, Narrative Evaluation, Timetable, Assessment Windows) re-run clean, since none of them assert on checkbox pixel size, only on functional behaviour, which this change does not touch.

Made the partial self-contained (`@once`-guarded `<style>` block, safe even where both partials do co-occur). Added an optional `$filterable` flag (only turned on from the full-page Performance Dashboard context, the compact dashboard widget stays as-is): a live client side name/class filter box and a sort dropdown (lowest average first / name / class), all in vanilla JS, no reload.

### Confirm & Renew: reconcile verified subscription payments and advance the due date (2026-07-19)
- Status: Done
- Notes: live user report: "a school due was formerly 160000 but the plan changed and the new amount due happens to be 256000 which the school paid twice [in two separate transfers that together equal it]. there should be a way for the platform owner to confirm and renew the school plan." Confirmed against the real `greenfield` school's real data: two verified payments (₦160,000 + ₦96,000) summed to exactly the new ₦256,000 amount due, but neither `verify()`-ing a payment nor anything else in the existing flow ever advanced `next_billing_date` or reinstated a billing-locked school, that only ever happened through the generic, unrelated "Extend by N days" action.
- `subscription_payments` gained `applied_to_renewal_at` (nullable timestamp): a verified payment is "applied" the moment an admin uses it toward a renewal, so a later renewal's own "amount received" total can never double count a payment an earlier renewal already consumed. `SubscriptionService::verifiedUnappliedTotal()` sums whatever is verified and not yet applied. A new "Confirm & Renew" card (shown only when that total is greater than zero) on the admin Subscription detail page shows the real total next to the real amount due, an editable due date (defaulting to +90 days), and one action (`SubscriptionController::renew()`, `SubscriptionEvent::TYPE_RENEWED`) that atomically marks every unapplied verified payment as applied, moves `next_billing_date` forward, and — since confirming payment is by definition settling up — reinstates the school if it had been locked out for billing, all in one click instead of a separate "Restore access" afterward.
- QA: `SubscriptionTest` grew from 18 to 20 tests (added: two verified payments summing to a changed amount due are both applied and the due date advances on Confirm & Renew, confirming also reinstates a billing-locked school and does not re-offer an already-applied payment on a later renewal). Verified directly against `greenfield`'s real production-shaped data via tinker before writing the fix. All green.

### Report card custom text blocks and charts (2026-07-19)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user request on the same designer thread: "also add option to add text, charts etc to the report card and which can allow me allow something like addresss etc." A school's own free text (an address line, a disclaimer, a scholarship note) did not fit the fixed field catalog at all, since that catalog only ever describes real, structured data columns (name, class, admission number, ...), not arbitrary prose.
- **Text blocks**: new tenant table `report_card_custom_blocks` (label + content, a school-wide library, not tied to any one report card), referenced from the Layout Designer's existing body-section machinery as key `"custom_{id}"` (`ReportCardCustomBlock::bodyKey()`). `ReportCardLayout::isValidBodyKey()` now accepts either a fixed catalog key or that pattern, so a custom block behaves exactly like `remarks`/`signoff`/every other body section: draggable, reorderable, alignable, removable, all through the same tray/toolbar UI, no separate mechanism needed. A new "Custom text blocks" management card on the Layout Designer page (its own small forms, outside the main layout form) lets an admin add/edit/remove blocks; deleting one also strips it out of any saved layout that had it placed. Content renders through Blade's default `{{ }}` escaping, never raw HTML.
- **Charts**: "which data, which chart type" was genuinely ambiguous, so this was the one thing this session asked the user directly rather than guessing (AskUserQuestion) — answered "both": a Score Trend Chart (this one student's own overall average, one point per term, computed in `ReportCardService::overallTrend()` by averaging every subject's own `previous_terms` per term name then appending the current term's already-computed overall average) and a Subject Breakdown Chart (a bar per subject, this term's total out of 100, straight from the existing subjects table data, no new computation needed). Both are two more entries in the same `report_card_body_sections` catalog, print safe inline SVG (explicit hex colors for ink/muted text, never the theme-following `var(--ink)`/`var(--mut)` CSS custom properties the dashboard's own trend widget uses, since a printed/PDF report card must read correctly regardless of the viewer's on-screen theme; `var(--accent)` is still used for the line/bars, the school's own brand colour, consistent with every other accent usage already on the card).
- Issues/bugs found while building: the same Blade adjacent-directive compiler quirk recurred twice more in the two new chart partials (`@if` immediately followed by block-form `@php` with no blank line; `@foreach` immediately followed by block-form `@php` with no blank line), both fixed the same two ways already established this session, adding a blank line where the computation had to stay separate, and inlining the per-iteration math directly into the SVG attribute echoes where it didn't.
- QA: `ReportCardLayoutDesignerTest` grew by 3 cases (creating a text block, placing it, and seeing it render on the real report card end to end; deleting a placed block also removes it from the saved layout; both charts render real `<svg>`/`<polyline>`/`<rect>` markup on the real report card). Full `ReportCard*` suite re-run clean.

### Audit log hardening: tamper-evident hash chain, verify, and durable export (2026-07-20)
- Status: Done (application-code scope; see the explicit out-of-scope item below)
- Notes: user directive, given the production-audit-log-security checklist verbatim ("append-only, immutable storage, cryptographic signing/hashing, restrict direct DB access, ship to external logging systems") — "IMPLEMENT THESE." `App\Models\AuditLog` was already append-only by design (`booted()` throws on `updating`/`deleting`, tracker/CLAUDE.md already documented this); the four remaining asks split cleanly into two buildable-in-app pieces and one that plainly is not, handled honestly rather than faked:
  1. **Cryptographic hash chain** (new): `App\Services\Auditing\IntegrityChain::hashFor()` is the one place the algorithm lives — a sha256 of a row's own fields (action, subject, old/new values, actor, ip, user agent, url, timestamp) concatenated with the previous row's own hash, so altering any stored row by any means other than the immutable model's own insert path (a raw SQL `UPDATE`, the exact attack this defends against) changes what the *next* row's hash should have been, breaking the chain from that point forward. `Recorder::write()` now computes and stores `hash`/`previous_hash` on every new row (`hash`/`previous_hash` columns added via new platform + tenant migrations, each school's own audit_logs table gets its own independent chain, same isolation-even-for-logs principle as the table itself). `App\Services\Auditing\IntegrityChecker::verify()` walks a connection's chain in id order and recomputes/compares every row, used by both `php artisan audit-logs:verify` (loops platform + every provisioned school, non-zero exit on any break so cron can alert, scheduled daily at 05:00) and a new "Verify integrity" button on the admin Audit Log browser (platform connection only, on demand). `php artisan audit-logs:build-chain` backfills the hash chain onto every pre-existing row (idempotent, only touches rows where `hash IS NULL`, safe to re-run e.g. after a new school is provisioned) — run once against every local school and the platform DB as part of this build.
  2. **Durable / external-shippable export** (new): `php artisan audit-logs:export` (scheduled hourly) batches every audit_logs row written since the last run into an append-only, never-overwritten NDJSON file (one JSON object per line, the standard shape any external log shipper or SIEM tails) on `config('skulos.audit_export_disk')` (env `AUDIT_LOG_EXPORT_DISK`, defaults to the `local` private disk, same "admin/ops configures the real destination, nothing fabricated" pattern as SMTP settings). A watermark (highest exported `audit_logs.id`) is kept per connection via the existing `PlatformSetting`/tenant `Setting` key/value stores, so re-running never re-exports a row already shipped. Honesty caveat, documented in the config file's own comment: this is durable, not automatically immutable — genuine write-once-read-many storage requires pointing the disk at an S3 bucket with Object Lock/versioning turned on, which is an infrastructure/bucket-policy setting outside this codebase, not something application code can enable on your behalf.
  3. **Restricting direct database access**: explicitly **not implemented in code**, because it cannot be — this is a question of who holds real production database credentials (cPanel/MySQL user grants, VPN/bastion access, etc.), a deployment/ops policy, not a Laravel feature. Recorded here as a deployment runbook item rather than silently skipped or faked: before go-live, the production DB user the app connects as should not be the same account used for interactive admin access, and interactive access should go through a narrow, logged path (not a shared root credential).
- Bug found and fixed while running the backfill against real local data (2026-07-20, ~3,000 real platform audit rows from this session's own work): `AuditLogsBuildChain`'s first version used plain `chunk()` over a `whereNull('hash')` query while the loop itself was busy setting `hash` on each row it processed — since each chunk shrinks the very result set `chunk()`'s OFFSET-based paging depends on, later pages silently skipped roughly half the backlog (observed directly: 1400 rows expected, only 800 processed in one pass). Fixed by switching to `chunkById()`, which pages by the row's own id instead of OFFSET and is unaffected by the filtered column changing mid-loop. Also switched from one DB commit per row to one per 200-row chunk (wrapped in `DB::connection($connection)->transaction()`), the accidental discovery that motivated timing the two runs in the first place: the row-per-commit version processed roughly 500 rows/minute against SQLite, the batched version finished the entire remaining backlog in seconds.
- A second, related bug found the same way (running the real backfill, not the test suite): the very first `platform` and `qa-academy` backfill attempts ran before the `chunkById()` fix above was written, so their first ~200 rows were correctly chained by pure luck (small enough to fit one un-skipped chunk) before the OFFSET-skip bug kicked in and left rows 201+ null; the second, fixed run then correctly chained everything from row 201 onward but anchored its very first `previous_hash` to the highest already-chained row's hash (at the time, a row far later in the table, from a different chunk of the original buggy pass), not row 200's, the row truly before it in id order. Caught immediately by the newly built `audit-logs:verify`, which flagged exactly `audit_logs.id=201` as broken on both connections, before this was ever mistaken for working. Fixed by resetting `hash`/`previous_hash` to null for both connections (a raw update, the same sanctioned exception documented in `AuditLogsBuildChain`) and re-running the now-correct command fresh; every other school's database was only ever touched by the fixed command in one pass and verified clean the first time. Left here as the exact reason the "backfill, then verify, don't just assume" two-step exists as a real workflow, not a formality: a plausible-looking backfill can still be silently wrong, and only re-deriving the chain from scratch catches it.
- QA: new `AuditLogIntegrityTest` (14 cases): sequential real writes form a verifiable chain; each row's `previous_hash` matches the row before it and the first row's is null; a raw SQL tamper is caught by `IntegrityChecker` and pinpoints the exact tampered row id; the `audit-logs:verify` command exits 0 clean / 1 broken; `audit-logs:build-chain` backfills legacy null-hash rows into a valid chain and is idempotent on a second run; two schools' chains are verified fully independent of each other; `audit-logs:export` writes a real NDJSON batch matching the true row count and advances the watermark, a second run with no new activity produces no new file; the admin UI verify action flashes success/failure correctly for both a clean and a broken chain. Ran the real backfill against every local school (`qa-academy`, `greenfield`, `test`, `test2`, `skultest`, `data-s`, `training`, `debugnav`, hundreds of thousands of rows each from this session's own extensive demo-data seeding, since audit logs are intentionally exempt from the retention/purge command) and the platform DB, then, after finding and fixing the fragmented-chain bug above, `audit-logs:verify` with no `--connection` (the full, every-school sweep) confirmed every connection genuinely clean, not just the test suite's synthetic data.

### Platform-wide searchable/type-to-filter dropdown, plus two real cascading-select bugs (2026-07-20)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user directive: every long list anywhere in the app (country/state/LGA, staff/student/parent/role pickers, any other long `<select>`) should get a type-to-filter combobox instead of a plain native dropdown a person has to scroll through, applied platform-wide with no per-page wiring. Built as one progressive-enhancement script in `layouts/app.blade.php`: on `DOMContentLoaded`, every `<select>` with more than `SS_THRESHOLD` (8) options is wrapped in a custom combobox (a button showing the current selection plus a search-and-list panel), the original `<select>` stays in the DOM, visually hidden (`opacity:0`, `pointer-events:none`, kept for real form submission), so this is a pure enhancement over the real field, not a replacement of it. Opting a specific select out (`data-no-search`) and leaving short selects (status filters, term pickers, three way toggles) alone were both deliberate, search would be friction there, not help. `window.SkulosSearchable.enhanceAll(root)` is exposed globally so any script that repopulates a select's options after page load can re-run enhancement on it.
- Verification surfaced a real, reproducible bug in exactly the two places the user explicitly named (country/state/LGA and a similarly-shaped picker): `GeoPicker`'s state and LGA `<select>`s start with only a placeholder option (too few to enhance at page load), then get populated via `fetch()` once a country/state is chosen — nothing ever re-ran the enhancer afterward, so they silently stayed plain native dropdowns forever on every create-new-record form (the far more common path than editing a record with a country already chosen, which is the one case that happened to enhance correctly at load time). The same shape of bug was also found, unprompted, in the Finance payment form's fee-item-to-installment cascading picker. Both fixed the same way: call `window.SkulosSearchable.enhanceAll(select.parentNode)` right after the AJAX-populated `<select>`'s new options are appended; the call is a no-op for a select the enhancer already wrapped (guarded by a `data-ss-enhanced` flag), so it is safe to call on every repopulation, not just the first.
- QA: manually traced the enhance/open/filter/select/close lifecycle end to end, including the `MutationObserver` that keeps the toggle's displayed label in sync with a select repopulated while its own panel is closed, and confirmed `buildOptions()` re-reading `select.options` fresh on every panel open means a stale in-panel list from before an AJAX repopulation is never shown. No automated test coverage added (this is unauthenticated-DOM/JS behavior with no server round trip to assert against); the two real bugs found were caught by manual verification, not a test suite gap.

### UI/UX audit fix batch: bulk actions on every remaining list screen (2026-07-19 to 2026-07-20)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user directive: "NOW DO A DETAILED AUDIT OF THE ui ux, ANY MISSING MENU OR DESIGN ETC," delegated to a background research agent with a detailed brief (nav completeness, bulk-action coverage, design-system spot checks, icon monotony, grid/flex-wrap orphan risk). The agent found, among other things, 17 screens with real per-row actions (Suspend/Archive/Delete/Approve/Reject/Restore/Remove/...) but no way to act on more than one row at a time, a direct violation of the project's own standing "every list screen gets bulk actions" rule. Confirmed via "FIX ALL" (repeated, emphatic) and a follow-up re-check ("HAS EVERYTHING BEEN COVERED") that caught one item missing from the first pass (`admin/trial-requests.blade.php`).
- Every screen retrofitted with the same established Houses-index bulk-action pattern (checkbox-first `<th>`/`<td>`, an out-of-band `<form>` outside the `<table>` since per-row single-action forms already live inside `<td>`s and HTML forms cannot nest, an action bar of buttons below the table, vanilla JS select-all plus a "tick at least one first" submit guard): `admin/schools-index.blade.php`, `admin/trashed.blade.php` (bulk restore), `admin/subscriptions/show.blade.php` (bulk verify/reject pending payments), `admin/school.blade.php` (admins sub-list), `admin/trial-requests.blade.php`, `school/campuses/index.blade.php`, `school/calendar/index.blade.php`, `school/assignments/index.blade.php`, `school/academics/subjects.blade.php`, `school/examinations/grading.blade.php` (grading bands, one bulk form per scale, scales themselves are cards not a table and are not bulk-eligible), `school/examinations/structure.blade.php` (CA components and Exam components as two independent bulk forms; the window-overrides bulk action already existed under Extension requests), `school/timetable/periods.blade.php`, `school/profile/approvals.blade.php` (profile change requests and forgot-password requests, both approve and reject), `school/report-cards/pending-review.blade.php` (approve, send back for correction with one shared reason, and nudge), `school/settings-academic-calendar.blade.php` (archived sessions). `school/backups.blade.php` (both the admin and school-facing versions) was checked and deliberately left alone: neither exposes a delete action at all, only Download and the inherently single/deliberate Restore, so there is no bulk-eligible action to add.
- Two screens (`school/profile/approvals.blade.php` and `school/report-cards/pending-review.blade.php`) needed a variant of the pattern: a "Reject" bulk action needs a shared reason (reused the existing single-row `prompt()`-for-a-reason flow, applied once to the whole selection) and, since a `<input type="checkbox">` can only ever belong to one `<form>` via the `form=""` attribute but the same selection has to feed either an Approve or a Reject action, the row checkboxes were left unattached to any form and each bulk button instead gathers the current selection into hidden inputs on the right form at click time. `school/report-cards/pending-review.blade.php`'s bulk Approve/Reject/Nudge also reuse the existing `TeacherScope` class-arm scoping `bulkAssignReview()` already established in the same controller, so a scoped teacher's bulk selection is silently narrowed to their own classes rather than acted on for a class they cannot see, consistent with `StudentController::bulkAction()`'s own convention.
- Also cleaned up as a related, explicitly flagged item from the same audit: 13 files (not the 11 the audit's own confidence-limited scan found) had a now-dead `style="width:auto"` inline override on a checkbox or radio input, harmless leftovers from before the global checkbox-sizing fix (2026-07-19 Build Log entry above) made every plain checkbox/radio correctly sized app-wide on its own. Removed from all 13: `academics/index.blade.php`, `admin/broadcast.blade.php`, `admin/idle-timeout-settings.blade.php`, `calendar/edit.blade.php`, `calendar/create.blade.php`, `campuses/index.blade.php`, `houses/assign.blade.php`, `settings-retention.blade.php`, `settings-idle-timeout.blade.php` (two), `settings-communication.blade.php` (two), `setup.blade.php`, `students/import.blade.php` (two), `students/show.blade.php` (two).
- QA: new `UiAuditBulkActionsTest` (12 cases, one per newly added bulk controller method that did not already have a Houses-style bulk test elsewhere): schools bulk suspend/delete and bulk restore from the archive (admin); subjects bulk-detach; grading bands bulk-destroy; assessment components bulk-destroy correctly skips a component that already has a score against it rather than failing the whole batch; timetable periods bulk-destroy; academic sessions bulk-unarchive; profile change requests bulk-approve and bulk-reject (proving the shared-reason validation actually requires a reason); report card reviews bulk-approve and bulk-send-back (proving a student with no assigned reviewer is skipped, not force-assigned or 500ing); and a scoped teacher correctly 403s on a bulk action they have no permission for. Full backend regression suite re-run after this whole batch (see the entry below for the exact result). Every new bulk route was placed with the project's established "register the literal bulk-action route before any same-position `{id}` wildcard sharing the same HTTP verb" rule in mind; caught and fixed one real instance of getting this backwards while building it (`school.academics.subjects.bulk-detach`, a POST route registered after the existing POST `subjects.update`'s wildcard `{subjectId}` route, which would have silently swallowed every bulk-detach request as an attempted single-subject update with `subjectId="bulk-detach"` before this controller-level fix ever ran) before it ever reached a real request.

### Marketing site reads live pricing from the plans table (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user directive ("let's work on these," selecting this specific item after being asked which of the four remaining pending items to start on). The one still-open marketing site action item from tracker section 13: `site/pricing.html` and `site/index.html`'s pricing grids hardcoded price and a per-plan feature checklist by hand, kept in sync with the real `plans` table only by whoever remembered to re-copy it after an admin changed something on the Billing screen. `site/` is a separately hosted static GitHub Pages deploy with no server side rendering of its own (`.github/workflows/pages.yml` uploads it as-is, no build step), so "read live from the plans table" has to mean a client side fetch to the Laravel backend, not a template read.
- New `PlanCatalogController` (`GET /plans`, no domain constraint or auth, same reasoning as the existing `GeoController`) returns every active plan ordered by `sort_order`, each with its price, `is_purchasable` flag, and its raw `features` keys expanded and grouped into the same category/label shape `config('skulos.plan_feature_catalog')` already defines for the admin Billing screen's own checklist, plus `config('skulos.plan_universal_inclusions')`. New `config/cors.php` (this app had never needed one before; `HandleCors` is in Laravel 12's default middleware stack but resolves to a no-op with no published config) scoped as narrowly as possible: only the `plans` path, `GET` only, open origin (safe, since this is public read only marketing data with no cookies/session involved).
- New `site/assets/pricing.js`, shared by both HTML pages (previously each duplicated its own copy of the "See all features" modal wiring inline): on load, fetches `/plans` and, if it succeeds, overwrites each tier's price text, flips its purchasable state (removing the "Coming soon" flag and swapping "Join the waitlist" for "Start free term" when a plan the static HTML still marks as coming soon is actually purchasable now, confirmed against the real local dev database: Professional and Enterprise are both already `is_purchasable=true` there, ahead of the still-`false` `PlanSeeder` default, an admin having flipped them at some point this session, exactly the kind of drift this feature exists to stop), and replaces the feature-checklist modal's data source. Deliberately fails closed: the embedded `<script type="application/json" id="planFeaturesData">` blob each page already had stays in place as a fallback, and if the fetch errors for any reason (backend not deployed, offline, wrong API host, CORS not yet allowing the calling origin), the page is left exactly as its own static markup already has it rather than showing broken or blank pricing. The production API host is a `// TODO` left honestly unset in `pricing.js` (fetch simply no-ops without it) rather than a guessed or fabricated domain, since no live backend deployment exists yet (tracker's own "What's actually left to build" list) — filling it in is a one-line change once that happens.
- QA: new `PlanCatalogApiTest` (5 cases: every active plan returned in `sort_order`, correctly grouped, with the right purchasable flags; a category the plan has zero features in is omitted entirely rather than sent as an empty group; an inactive plan is excluded; universal inclusions are included; no authentication required). Verified the real endpoint directly over HTTP against the real local dev database (`curl` with a cross origin `Origin` header, confirming a real `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` response header and the exact live JSON shape `pricing.js` expects), started a throwaway static file server for `site/` and confirmed both HTML pages load with the new `pricing.js` include and no syntax errors (`node -c`), then stopped it. Full backend regression suite re-run clean after this change (893 passed, matching the count after the weekend-date fix above, i.e. this feature broke nothing and needed no new failures fixed). Could not visually confirm the live price/modal swap rendering in an actual browser, no browser/screenshot tool available in this environment (same limitation noted on the Guided Setup icon fix, 2026-07-16); verification instead traced the real API response by hand against `pricing.js`'s logic line by line to confirm no runtime error and the exact expected DOM changes.

### Idle timeout countdown visually freezing (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done
- Notes: live user report, a screenshot of the "Are you still there?" dialog stuck showing "30" ("countdown stuck here"), then confirmed on follow up that the real security timeout still fired correctly on schedule ("it stuck for a while but the screen did time out") — a display bug, not a security one. Root cause: the countdown redraw ran on `setInterval(evaluate, 1000)`; `setInterval` keeps firing on its own fixed schedule regardless of how long a previous tick took to actually run, so ticks pile up and visibly lag under main thread contention (a heavy fetch, a big DOM update elsewhere on the page, a throttled background tab), even though `evaluate()` itself was always correct either way, it computes the remaining seconds from an absolute `warningDeadlineAt` timestamp, never a decrementing counter, so the actual logout timing was never wrong.
- Fixed by switching to a self correcting recursive `setTimeout`: `tick()` calls `evaluate()` then reschedules itself off the real current time, so a late tick corrects itself on the next one instead of compounding, rather than `setInterval`'s fixed schedule silently falling behind.
- QA: no automated coverage added, this is unauthenticated client side timing/rendering behavior with no server round trip to assert against and this repo has no JS test runner (same limitation as the sidebar scroll fix, 2026-07-11); verified by reading the before/after logic by hand to confirm `evaluate()`'s own correctness was unchanged and only the redraw scheduling mechanism differs.

### Impersonation ("View as this admin") silently losing the session across the subdomain boundary (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done
- Notes: live user report immediately after the countdown fix above ("View as this admin is not working... it navigates to localhost:8000 and show This page isn't working", then on retry "shwoing 404... when i try to access as the admin now"). Root cause, found by direct investigation (server logs, `route()` output via tinker, and a real `SESSION_DOMAIN`/cookie-scoping check) rather than guesswork: this app deliberately runs with `SESSION_DOMAIN=null` (host-only session cookies) — the tracker's own 2026-07-08 "Clickable platform UI" entry already documents that a shared leading-dot `SESSION_DOMAIN=.localhost` was tried on day one and reverted, since it made the browser reject the cookie outright on the bare `localhost` host, 419ing every login. `impersonate()` logged the target user in and set `session('impersonator_id')` on the **platform admin's own bare-domain host**, then redirected the browser to the **school's subdomain**, a genuinely different host from the browser's point of view; the just-set, host-only cookie was never sent there, so the destination page loaded fully signed out, 404ing on `EnsureSchoolMember`'s "no user" guard (or worse, briefly appearing to work then silently dropping the impersonated session). `endImpersonation()` had the identical bug in reverse (school subdomain to the bare domain), never actually reported live but found by the same analysis and confirmed broken the same way once tested.
- **A mid-investigation self-inflicted regression, caught and fixed before it reached the user again**: while diagnosing, briefly set `SESSION_DOMAIN=.localhost` in the real local `.env` to test whether the tracker's day-one finding still held (it does — that fix was never actually deployed, so a fresh browser profile hitting bare `localhost` while a leading-dot cookie was active anywhere during that window got its Set-Cookie rejected). Reverted within the same turn once the tracker's prior entry was found, but the brief window still corrupted whatever cookie the user's live browser held at that moment, cascading into "so many pages showing 404" — resolved by restarting the dev server clean (ruling out a stale server-side process) and having the user clear cookies for `localhost` in their regular browser profile (confirmed via "works in incognito," i.e. a profile with no old cookie at all), not a code fix, a real lesson in not testing an already-documented-as-broken config change against the live environment first.
- **The actual fix**: neither direction logs in directly and redirects cross host anymore. `impersonate()` now redirects to a **signed URL** (`URL::temporarySignedRoute`, 2 minute expiry) pointing at a new route that runs **on the destination host itself** (`school.impersonate.consume`, sat inside the school subdomain's own route group, deliberately outside both the `guest` and `auth` middleware groups there since the browser is authenticated on neither host yet at that point, protected by the `signed` middleware alone) — that route does the actual `Auth::login()` there, so the session cookie that gets set is correctly scoped to the host it is about to be used on. `endImpersonation()` mirrors this in reverse: logs the school-scoped session out cleanly, then hands off via another signed URL to a new `session.resume-admin` route on the bare domain, which logs the admin back in fresh there. No `SESSION_DOMAIN` change needed at all, and none was made — Laravel's built-in signed URL primitive (tamper proof via `APP_KEY`, time limited) is exactly a cross host handoff token, no new storage or config required.
- **A second, unrelated bug found and fixed while building this**: `consumeImpersonation(Request $request, User $user, Recorder $recorder)`'s `User $user` type hint threw `must be of type App\Models\User, string given` instead of resolving via route model binding — a route parameter on a `Route::domain('{school}.'.$domain)`-wildcarded route not binding correctly through an Eloquent type hint, a stronger form of the domain-routing gotcha CLAUDE.md already documents for scalar params (that entry claims Eloquent-typed params are immune; this shows that is not reliably true for every domain+path-parameter combination). Fixed the proven way already established elsewhere in this codebase for exactly this situation: read `$request->route('user')` and look the model up explicitly, rather than relying on implicit binding.
- **A third bug found while fixing the second**: `layouts/app.blade.php`'s impersonate banner called `route('session.end-impersonation')` with no parameters; once that route moved inside the school subdomain's route group it required a `school` domain parameter the shared layout (used by both the admin and school portals) cannot always supply. Fixed by using a plain relative form action (`/session/end-impersonation`) instead of `route()` — the browser already submits to whatever host the page is actually on, which is exactly the one host this route needs to run on.
- QA: `SchoolAdminManagementTest`'s impersonation tests rewritten for the two-hop flow (follow the signed handoff URL as a real second request rather than asserting a direct redirect), plus a new case proving a tampered signed URL (a different target user id spliced into an otherwise-valid signed URL) is rejected 403 rather than silently impersonating someone else. Confirmed the four new/moved routes (`school.impersonate.consume`, `session.end-impersonation`, `session.resume-admin`, plus the unchanged `admin.schools.admins.impersonate`) via `php artisan route:list` are registered on the correct hosts. Full backend regression suite re-run clean (899 passed, up from 898, the one new tamper test).

New `Admin\KnowledgeBaseController` (category/article CRUD, gated `super_admin`, same "admin CMS" shape as the Marketing Pages editor) and `School\KnowledgeBaseController` (browse/search published articles, gated only on being a signed in member of the school, no dedicated permission, since a help resource should be readable by every role, not managed by most of them).

New `admin.knowledge-base.index` link added to the platform admin nav.

Full backend regression suite re-run clean.

### Domain aware guest redirect, a real pre-existing bug surfaced by the idle timeout (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done
- Notes: live user report right after successfully using the app for a while: the idle timeout countdown got stuck again (this time the real timeout also failed to fire, not just the display, "did not log out"), then after being asked to hard refresh, "the page just keeps reloading without loading the actual page" on every URL tried, including a fresh incognito window, so not a stale browser cache issue.
- Root cause, confirmed directly by following the real redirect chain over HTTP (`curl -v -L`) rather than guessed at: an unauthenticated request to a protected route on a school's own subdomain (exactly what happens once a session genuinely expires, whether via the idle timeout or anything else) was redirecting to `http://localhost:8000/login`, the platform admin's own bare domain sign-in page, not the school's own branded `{slug}.localhost:8000/login`. Laravel's default guest-redirect behavior just calls `route('login')` unconditionally, the one route literally named that, with no awareness of which host the original request actually came in on. This is a genuinely pre-existing gap, not something introduced this session, it had simply never been exercised live before: normal testing is almost always already authenticated when browsing, so "guest hits a protected school subdomain route" had never actually happened in front of a real browser until a session organically expired during this session's testing.
- This plausibly explains the reload loop itself, not just a wrong page: a stale but still valid platform admin session cookie sitting on the bare `localhost` host (left over from all the admin portal testing this session) could make that wrong login page look "already signed in enough" to try bouncing the browser back to the originally intended school subdomain URL, which still is not authenticated there, bouncing back to the wrong login again, and so on.
- Fixed in `bootstrap/app.php` via Laravel 12's `$middleware->redirectGuestsTo()` hook: a closure reads `$request->route('school')` (the domain wildcard already resolved for the current request) and, if present, redirects to `school.login` for that slug; otherwise falls back to the plain `login` route exactly as before, so the platform admin portal's own behavior is unchanged.
- QA: new `DomainAwareGuestRedirectTest` (2 cases: an unauthenticated request to a provisioned school's subdomain redirects to that same school's own login, not the bare domain; an unauthenticated request to the platform admin portal still redirects to the bare domain login exactly as before). Confirmed directly against the real running dev server over HTTP before and after the fix (`curl`, the wrong host in the `Location` header before, the correct one after). Full backend regression suite re-run clean (906 passed), confirming this global change to every protected route's unauthenticated behavior broke nothing else.

The user's "FEEDBACK LIST.docx" got a first "16 issues" pass and a newly appended "6 items" 7/22/2026 section, both processed in the same run (kept here as one batch since most items are small, independent fixes rather than one feature). The tracker itself was deliberately held back mid-batch ("dont touch my feedback list yet, i will let you know when to do so but do commit and push everything"): every item below shipped, was tested, and was pushed commit by commit at the time (see `git log 624a935..f94fe6d`), this write-up is the deferred documentation pass, done now that the user said go. Full regression suite at the end of this whole batch: **958 passed, 0 failed** (up from 906 at the last tracker checkpoint).

### Env/config documentation gap (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done
- Notes: `APP_DOMAIN`, the tenancy driver/prefix, support contact, subscription bank details, idle timeout, retention windows, audit export disk, and self-hosted licensing all had a real `env()` call reading them somewhere in `config/`, with no line in `.env.example` to make them discoverable. Settings that already have a safe code-level default are added commented out (showing that real default as the example value) so copying this file never silently changes runtime behaviour.
- QA: reviewed against every `env()` call in `config/skulos.php` and related config files to confirm nothing discoverable was missed.

### Idle timeout countdown freeze (second root cause) + three searchable-select bugs (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done
- Notes: `evaluate()` returned before ever updating the on-screen countdown once `secondsLeft` hit 0, so the modal visually froze while `doLogout()` kept re-firing on every tick with no way to stop. Added a `loggingOut` guard, a `tickRunning` flag with a `startTicking()` helper so `resetIdleTimer()` can reliably restart a stopped cycle, and a CSRF token refresh immediately before the logout form submits.
- Three separate live bug reports against the platform-wide searchable/type-to-filter combobox (built 2026-07-20): multiple panels could be open at once (no shared registry to close siblings), a panel's `hidden` attribute never actually hid it because `.ss-panel`'s own `display:flex` tied with the UA `[hidden]` rule and won on source order, and clicking an option just closed the panel without selecting because `mousedown` on the option blurred the still-focused search input before the click registered. Fixed with a shared `ssInstances` registry plus one document click listener, a more specific `.ss-panel[hidden]` rule, and `preventDefault` on option `mousedown`. Also fixed the search icon overlapping input text in the same component: the global `input:not():not()` field rule outranked `.ss-search input`'s padding-left by CSS specificity.
- Issues/Bugs: this is the second of what became a three-pass fix on the idle timeout countdown this session; see the "Idle logout in a backgrounded tab" entry below for the third and actual root cause of the underlying complaint.
- QA: manual verification (no JS test runner in this repo, an existing documented limitation); full backend suite unaffected (server-side behaviour untouched).

- Notes: a school that had never visited Timetable > Periods hit a hard abort the moment it picked a class, a completely normal, anticipated state rather than an error. Now redirects to Periods setup with a status message, matching the graceful "nothing set up yet" pattern every other screen in the app already uses.

### Class streams shown as indented sub-rows, not folded into the parent (2026-07-21)

Triggered on arm count rather than whether an arm happens to be named, so a legacy unnamed arm from before this convention existed still surfaces as its own row instead of silently disappearing. Applied to both Classes & Subjects and the student browse-by-class screen.

### Platform database backup, separate from per-school backups (2026-07-21)
- Status: Done

Create/download/delete only, no restore via the UI: restoring the platform DB the app is actively reading from mid-request is materially riskier than restoring one tenant DB the app can safely disconnect from, so restore stays a documented manual, app-offline operation. Scheduled daily at 02:30 via a new `platform:backup` command, plus a manual "back up now" button on the platform admin screen.
- QA: command run manually against the real local platform DB, download and delete verified end to end.

Staff Portal already links out to a flat "Full staff list" alongside its own section/class browse; Parent Portal had no equivalent, only the class browse. Added `ParentDirectoryController::all()`, a searchable, paginated flat list of every guardian record, each row linking to the guardian's student where guardian management already lives (browse-only here, consistent with the existing "don't duplicate management" stance). One row per `StudentGuardian` record rather than a deduplicated person, since two rows for the same real parent across siblings can legitimately carry different relationship/contact details.

Added a "Report card" button next to Edit, shown when a current term exists and the viewer holds `report_cards.view`.

### Admin-defined custom fields for Students, Staff, and Parents (2026-07-22)
- Status: Done
- Notes: standing item, un-deferred this pass: "I WANT THE ADMIN TO BE ABLE TO CREATE A NEW FIELD THAT IS NOT THERE BEFORE AND ASSIGN TO STUDENTS, STAFF AND PARENTS." A genuinely new field can't mean a schema migration per school per field, so this is a standard EAV pair (`custom_field_definitions`, `custom_field_values`) instead: an admin defines a field once (label, type: text/textarea/number/date/dropdown/checkbox, optional required flag) from Settings > Custom Fields, picks which of Student/Staff/Parent it applies to, and it shows up on that entity's create/edit form and profile page. Guardian values are keyed off the individual `StudentGuardian` row, consistent with how guardian data already works elsewhere in this app (no first-class "person" model). Management screen gated on `school_info.manage_settings`, the same tier as every other school-configuration screen, with the standard bulk activate/deactivate/delete actions. Deleting a definition cascades its values; deactivating one preserves them without showing or requiring the field going forward.
- QA: new feature test suite covering definition CRUD, per-entity-type scoping, value save/display on all three entity types, and bulk actions. This EAV shape (`entity_type`/`entity_id`, a plain column pair rather than a real polymorphic relation) was later reused as the direct precedent for the Examination Timetable's own `scope_type`/`scope_id` pointer, see that entry.

### Header notification bell: a real dropdown (2026-07-22)
- Status: Done
- Notes: the bell icon in the app header was a static, unclickable SVG that only even rendered on the dashboard (it depended on a `$notifCount` variable only that one controller ever passed in). It's now a real `<details>` dropdown on every page, querying its own small recent-notifications feed the same way `broadcast-popup.blade.php` already queries its own data inline. Mark as read (both here and on the dashboard's own Notifications widget) now fires via `fetch()` instead of a submitting `<form>`, so it no longer reloads the page; the button/label reads "Mark as read" everywhere, not "Mark read" (a direct wording correction from the feedback doc).
- Issues/Bugs: two existing tests asserted a notification title never appears anywhere on a given page, an assumption that was only ever true because notifications had no global UI surface before this.
- Resolution: rescoped both tests to check the actual underlying list data instead of raw page text, which is what they actually meant to verify.
- QA: full suite re-run clean after the rescoping.

### Communication History: who has and hasn't acknowledged, plus a nudge, plus pagination (2026-07-22)
- Status: Done
- Notes: user request: "ONE SHOULD BE ABLE TO SEE LIST OF THOSE WHO ACKNOWLEDGE EACH COMMUNICATION SO AS TO ALSO NUDGE THOSE WHO ARE YET TO DO SO." Communication History already showed a "X of Y acknowledged" figure per item; there was no way to see who the X and Y actually were, or follow up with the ones still pending. The figure now links to a recipient list (acknowledged vs pending, with timestamps) and a "nudge everyone pending" action that sends each of them a fresh in-app reminder. The reminder deliberately carries no `announcement_id` of its own, since the ledger's own count is a `withCount()` over every notification row carrying that id; a reminder must not inflate it as if it were a second delivery of the original.
- Found during a later Dbase recommendation recompliance recheck the same session (not a user report): this recipient list loaded every recipient's `Notification` row, full `User` model eager loaded, in one unbounded query, fine for the small test data it shipped with but a real problem for a "whole school" broadcast, which can mean hundreds of recipients. Paginated (30/page) and scoped both queries to only the columns the view actually renders, matching the standing "paginate every list screen, select only needed columns" rule. The acknowledged/pending counts and the nudge action already ran their own independent queries, unaffected by paginating the display list.
- QA: new feature tests for the drill-down list, the nudge action (proving it does not inflate the ledger count and requires `communication.broadcast`), and, from the pagination fix, a 35-recipient case proving page 1 shows 30 and page 2 shows the remaining 5. Full `CommunicationHistoryTest` suite (9 cases) green.

### Idle logout in a backgrounded tab: the real, third-pass root cause (2026-07-22)
- Status: Done
- Notes: the countdown display no longer freezing (previous entry above) and an earlier double-logout-call fix didn't fix the actual complaint: "got to 0 and was stuck for a while... only log out when one go back to the tab or window." The real cause is that the logout itself only ever fired from `tick()`'s repeating 1-second `setTimeout` loop, and a repeating short timer in a backgrounded tab is exactly what Chrome (and others) throttle or fully suspend, sometimes for a minute or more. A single `setTimeout` scheduled for one precise delay is throttled far less aggressively than a tight repeating loop, so the moment the tab goes hidden (or the window loses focus, which doesn't always fire `visibilitychange` on its own), the 1-second loop is replaced by one exact-deadline timer instead; visibility/focus returning restores the normal visible countdown loop.
- QA: manual verification (no JS test runner in this repo); this closes out all three passes on the idle timeout report from this session (display freeze fix, then the loggingOut/tick-restart guard, now the actual backgrounded-tab timer fix).

### Report cards fit one A4 page when printed, plus bulk class print (2026-07-22)
